The Josephite Genesis

A reading text of Genesis with the layers identified as later removed

Dawn Youngblood, Ph.D.
Department of Anthropology, Southern Methodist University
dyoungbl@smu.edu
Preface I. Before the Fathers II. Abraham III. Isaac IV. Jacob V. Joseph Seams Apparatus

  • Framework numbers and headings 63

  • Southern addition 33
  • Lists of peoples 30
  • Glosses and blind-tested additions 12
  • Set aside, not tested 54

Hebrew: the Leningrad Codex (Westminster Leningrad Codex 4.20, Open Scriptures Hebrew Bible edition). Each Hebrew line gives the whole Masoretic verse. Where the English drops a phrase inside a verse, or reads “God” for YHWH or “the king” for Pharaoh, the Hebrew keeps the received wording. Ketiv appears in gray, with qere in brackets; פ and ס mark open and closed paragraphs.

What this text is

This is an experiment: Genesis with the layers that the project’s calibrated tests identified as later additions taken out, so that the remainder can be read as a continuous story. It is one reconstruction under stated rules, not a recovered document. Its value lies in how it reads, and in where it breaks.

The English wording is the World English Bible (WEB), a modern translation in the public domain. It was chosen because it may be freely copied, edited, and published, not because it is the best translation of Genesis. The tests did not rest on it. They were run on the Hebrew of the Leningrad Codex (1008 CE), the oldest complete manuscript of the Hebrew Bible, in two digital editions: the ETCBC’s BHSA database and the Westminster Leningrad Codex with the Open Scriptures morphology. The blind readers coded the Hebrew. Two steps used the English: the horizon audit located words in the WEB, and the Pharaoh test separated speech from narration by the WEB’s quotation marks. The test of the divine names compared the Hebrew with the Samaritan Pentateuch, the Qumran manuscripts, and the Septuagint.

The cuts follow content, not verse divisions. Verse division is scribal: the Mishnah already treats the verse as a reading unit, the Masoretes marked its end, and numbered verses entered printed Bibles only in the fifteenth and sixteenth centuries [verify]. Chapters are later still, the work of Stephen Langton in the early thirteenth century. The layers do not respect these units. A single verse of Gen 5 carries an old name and a framework age; Gen 2–3 joins YHWH and Elohim in one compound. The text below therefore removes phrases inside verses where the tests require it, and it prints without chapter or verse numbers. The button marked “Ch. & v.” restores them for checking.

An open experiment

Every decision in this text can be checked, and any of them can be overturned. Each removal, restoration, and mark rests on a stated rule and a named test. Two blind readers, given no hypothesis, coded passages as detachable, substitutable, or woven, with calibration controls drawn from documented additions such as the Chronicler’s reworking of Kings. Words and names were dated by their first attestation outside the Bible. An older word is restored only where the same story preserves it for the same referent. The tests, their scripts, and their results are published with the text. Some decisions are compromises, and the notes say so where they are; items marked [verify] await checking in print.

Readers and scholars are invited to comment, to propose better tests, and to point out errors. Three kinds of evidence would change a decision: an outside attestation that moves a word or name to an earlier or later horizon; a controlled blind reading that codes a passage differently; or a manuscript reading that alters the wording. Comments are welcome through the website where this text is posted, or by mail to Dawn Youngblood, Ph.D., Department of Anthropology, Southern Methodist University, dyoungbl@smu.edu.

What was replaced

Some later words did not join the old account beside its wording; they took the place of an older word. Where the tests show the older word, the text below restores it and marks the change, so that each restoration can be checked against the base translation.

  1. “Pharaoh” read as “the king” (※). Every “Pharaoh” of the base text reads “the king,” and “the king of Egypt” at the first mention in each story (12:15; 37:36). Four findings support the change.
    • The title is New Kingdom. Egyptian pr-ꜥꜣ, “Great House,” named the palace. It came to stand for the king himself in the Eighteenth Dynasty, possibly under Thutmose III (c. 1479–1425 BCE) and securely in a letter to Akhenaten (c. 1353–1336). It was first joined to a king’s personal name under Siamun of the Twenty-first Dynasty, in the tenth century [verify in print: Alan Gardiner, Egyptian Grammar, 3rd ed. (Oxford: Griffith Institute, 1957), 75]. A family memory of the Middle Bronze Age could not have called the king Pharaoh.
    • The older word survives. Of 83 places where the non-Priestly Joseph story names the Egyptian king, four say “king”: “the place where the king’s prisoners were bound” (ʾăsîrê hammelek, 39:20), and “the butler of the king of Egypt and his baker offended their lord, the king of Egypt” (40:1; again 40:5). All four fall within eight verses, a cluster that random scatter produces in 0.05 percent of trials. Exodus keeps the same pair in one episode: “the king of Egypt” speaks to the midwives (Exod 1:15, 17, 18), and they answer “Pharaoh” (1:19). Documented replacements leave a comparable remainder. The Elohistic Psalter keeps 40 of its YHWHs against 245 Elohim (14 percent), scattered through 24 psalms; Ps 53 replaces every YHWH of Ps 14. Second Samuel writes -bōšet for baʿal in all 27 of its Baal names (Ish-bosheth, Mephibosheth, Jerubbesheth), while Chronicles keeps Eshbaal and Merib-baal (1 Chr 8:33–34; 9:39–40). The Joseph story’s 5 percent sits inside that range. Its clustering also fits a seam between two tellings, one saying “king of Egypt” and one “Pharaoh”; either way, the older wording is “king.”
    • The idiom is Egyptian. Hebrew court narrative calls the reigning king “the king”: 85 percent of references in Esther, 77 percent in the Court History of David (2 Sam 13–20; 1 Kgs 1–2), 74 percent in Daniel 1. Courtiers speak of him in the third person as “my lord the king,” and swear “as my lord the king lives” (2 Sam 15:21; compare 1 Sam 17:55). The Joseph story fills those slots with “Pharaoh,” 30 times in speech: “Let Pharaoh look for a discreet and wise man” (41:33); “you are even as Pharaoh” (44:18); “by the life of Pharaoh” (42:15–16). Exodus 1–14 uses “Pharaoh” 110 times and “the king” not once. New Kingdom Egyptians spoke of the king in the same way, as “Pharaoh, life, prosperity, health,” in narration and in letters [verify passages: the Late Ramesside Letters, ed. Jaroslav Černý (Brussels: Fondation Égyptologique Reine Élisabeth, 1939); the Tale of the Two Brothers, Papyrus d’Orbiney]. Whoever wrote “Pharaoh” into these stories wrote as an Egyptian courtier of that age spoke.
    • The usage predates the tenth century. Genesis and Exodus never name a pharaoh and never join the title to a name. Kings follows the Egyptian change in order: the pharaohs of Solomon’s reign are “Pharaoh” or “Pharaoh king of Egypt,” unnamed (1 Kgs 3:1; 9:16; 11:18); Shishak and So are named as “king of Egypt” (1 Kgs 14:25; 2 Kgs 17:4); only the kings of the late seventh and sixth centuries are “Pharaoh Neco” and “Pharaoh Hophra” (2 Kgs 23:29–35; Jer 44:30; 46:2). A writer in the idiom of Kings would have named his pharaoh. The Pentateuch’s bare title belongs to the New Kingdom and the generations after it, which is Phase 2 of the project’s model.
    With the title restored, Joseph swears “by the life of the king,” the Hebrew oath. The tests do not decide whether a reteller substituted the title or whether two tellings met at 40:1–5; the change follows the same rule as the divine name, since the first phase predates the title. (Tests: pharaoh-replacement/results.md in the project.)
  2. The Philistines read as Gerar, and Joseph’s title “governor” read as “the lord of the land” (※). Both later terms have their older wording in the same stories, so the restoration is one for one. The Philistines enter the record with the Sea Peoples at Medinet Habu (Ramesses III, year 8, c. 1177 BCE), a date for the people’s arrival and not only for the name. Gen 20 tells its wife-sister story of “Abimelech king of Gerar” with no Philistines at all (20:2), and Isaac’s quarrel is with “the herdsmen of Gerar” (26:20) and “the men of the place” (26:7). The text now reads “returned to Gerar” (21:32), “lived as a foreigner in Gerar” (21:34, as 20:1), “Abimelech king of Gerar” (26:8, as 20:2), and “the men of the place envied him” (26:14, as 26:7). Joseph’s title šallîṭ (42:6) has its distribution in the Persian period (Qohelet, Ezra–Nehemiah, the Aramaic of Daniel), and the brothers call him “the man, the lord of the land” twice in the same chapter (42:30, 33); 42:6 now reads “Joseph was the lord of the land.”
  3. Bethel read as Luz in Abraham’s journeys (※). In the story’s own sequence the name Bethel does not yet exist when Abraham pitches his tent on the ridge (12:8; 13:3): Jacob gives it later, and “the name of the city was Luz at first” (28:19; also Judg 1:23; Josh 18:13). The sanctuary test of October 8 found that Jacob’s Bethel and Shechem scenes do not know Abraham was ever there, that Abraham’s northern presence is notices only (five verses, against full scenes at his southern sites), and that his northern altar notices share their formula with Isaac’s at Beersheba rather than with Jacob’s scenes, the mark of the linking layer that joins the cycles. Those altar notices are already removed. The route itself may be old, since a family coming from Haran enters by way of Shechem and the Bethel ridge, and the ridge sets the next scene, where Lot looks out over the plain of the Jordan (13:10). Two blind readers coded “Bethel before Jacob names it” as substitutable (October 6). The itinerary therefore keeps its route under the city’s older name: “east of Luz … Luz on the west, and Ai on the east” (12:8), “between Luz and Ai” (13:3). Bethel stands from Jacob’s naming onward.
  4. The court official sārîs read as “servant” (※). Hebrew sārîs renders Akkadian ša rēši, “the one at the head,” a royal attendant; the Hebrew form is usually taken as a loan from Neo-Assyrian [verify the earlier, Middle Assyrian use of ša rēši]. The Joseph story supplies the older word for the same men. The two officials the king put in custody are “his two sārîsîm” in 40:2 and “his servants” (ʿăbādāyw) when the cupbearer recounts the same event (41:10), and the feast of 40:20 is held “for all his servants.” The text now reads “his two servants” (40:2), “the king’s servants” (40:7), and, for Joseph’s first master, “a servant of the king of Egypt” (37:36). In blind set 4 the readers coded the word detachable or substitutable at all three places, never woven.
  5. The compound divine name. “Yahweh God,” confined to Gen 2:4–3:23 and probably an editorial bridge, is reduced to “God.”
  6. The divine name: Yahweh read as “God” (°). This text is set in the first phase, the family memory, which in the project’s model comes before the name YHWH. Every “Yahweh” of the base text therefore reads “God,” marked °, in narration and speech alike. Three findings support the change. God tells Moses that the fathers knew him as El Shaddai, “but by my name YHWH I was not known to them” (Exod 6:3), and in the Joseph story no character ever says YHWH. The name first appears outside the Bible in New Kingdom lists of the “land of the Shasu of yhw” near Seir (Soleb, Amenhotep III, c. 1390–1352 BCE; copied at Amara West under Ramesses II), the region where Exodus places Moses’s meeting with YHWH; whether yhw there names a god, a place, or a group is disputed. And the names inside the YHWH stories are built on El, while YHWH stands in the narration around them: Ishmael, “El hears,” explained by “YHWH has heard” (16:11); El-roi (16:13); “Elohim will provide” (22:8) beside the place called “YHWH will provide” (22:14); “the angel of Elohim” in the second Hagar story (21:17) for “the angel of YHWH” in the first. Genesis contains no personal name built on YHWH. The El epithets stay as they are. The ancient witnesses show how unsettled the two names were in Genesis. In the Septuagint about one YHWH in five stands as theos, “God,” against one in thirty in Deuteronomy; the Samaritan Pentateuch exchanges the names in both directions; and at 22:14 a Qumran manuscript, 4QGen-Exoda, reads “Elohim will provide,” matching Abraham’s words in 22:8 [verify DJD 12]. No witness reads “God” throughout, and in every one YHWH still dominates the Abraham and Isaac stories and Elohim the Jacob and Joseph stories.

What was removed

  1. Framework numbers and headings. Ages, life spans, dates, totals, and the tôlĕdôt headings. Across the Sumerian King List and the MT, SP, and LXX of Gen 5, numbers changed while names held. The name lists themselves stay, as lineages memorized by name; Gen 5 and 11 appear as lists of names. That is the form pastoral genealogies take in the ethnographic record: herders recite names, in several branching lineages, and attach no ages. Northern Somali herders learn their patrilines name by name, twenty or more generations back to a clan founder, and the lines branch into the lineages that order alliance and blood payment (I. M. Lewis, A Pastoral Democracy: A Study of Pastoralism and Politics among the Northern Somali of the Horn of Africa [London: Oxford University Press for the International African Institute, 1961]; Lewis, “Historical Aspects of Genealogies in Northern Somali Social Structure,” Journal of African History 3, no. 1 [1962]: 35–48). A Somali childhood memoir records the same practice: children reciting their ancestors by name (Shugri Said Salh, The Last Nomad: Coming of Age in the Somali Desert [New York: Workman, 2021]). Robert R. Wilson read the biblical lists against African and Arab lineage ethnography, separating the linear lines of Gen 5 and 11 from the segmented genealogy of Gen 10, each shaped by its social use (Genealogy and History in the Biblical World, Yale Near Eastern Researches 7 [New Haven: Yale University Press, 1977]). The two lists hold twenty generations from Adam to Abram: ten from Adam to Noah, ten from Shem to Abram. Twenty is the depth Lewis recorded among northern Somali herders reckoning back to a clan founder. Noth assigns both lists, with their ages, to the Priestly writer, so the break between them at the flood, two decads each closing on a father of three sons (Noah: Shem, Ham, and Japheth; Terah: Abram, Nahor, and Haran), belongs with the framework rather than with the recitation. The text below gives the twenty names once, in one place, as a herder would recite them, before the story of Abram (at 11:10–26), under the list’s own introduction from Gen 5:1–2 (“This is the book of the generations of Adam …”); Lamech’s naming of Noah (5:29) and Noah’s three sons (5:32) stay in the flood story, and Enoch’s note (5:24) follows the list. The Septuagint adds a Cainan between Arpachshad and Shelah (LXX Gen 11:12–13; followed in Luke 3:36), which would make twenty-one. Names held without numbers are also the old form of such genealogies in the Amorite world. The Assyrian King List opens with seventeen “kings who lived in tents,” named in sequence with no reign lengths; the section is generally traced to the ancestry of Šamši-Adad I in the eighteenth century, although the surviving copies date from the first millennium. The genealogy of the Hammurabi dynasty, an Old Babylonian text, shares several of its names (J. J. Finkelstein, “The Genealogy of the Hammurapi Dynasty,” Journal of Cuneiform Studies 20 [1966]: 95–118), and Abraham Malamat set both lists beside Gen 5 and 11 (“King Lists of the Old Babylonian Period and Biblical Genealogies,” Journal of the American Oriental Society 88 [1968]: 163–73) [verify pages]. The second of the tent-dwelling kings is Adamu. The name attests Adam as an ancestral name in that world; it does not make the two men one.
  2. Priestly framework and narrative, following Noth, except where it carries a legend, family law, or the only telling of an event. That retained material is marked ‡.
  3. The linking layer. Promise speeches of land, seed, and blessing; altar and invocation notices (except the altar on Moriah and Noah’s altar, which are woven into their scenes); notes that cross-reference one cycle from another; and forward links to the Exodus. With them goes Gen 15, the promise-and-covenant chapter.
  4. Glosses. “To this day” notes; the Canaanites “in the land at that time”; “of the Chaldees”; the origin of the Philistines; iron beside bronze; explanatory place names (Bethlehem for Ephrath, Hebron for Kiriath-arba, Luz for Bethel).
  5. Clean and unclean animals. The flood’s division of the animals into clean and unclean (7:2, 8; 8:20) is removed, with “by pairs” in 7:9, which harmonizes the seven pairs with the Priestly two of each. The flood now reads “seven pairs of every animal,” as the Masoretic text already reads for the birds (7:3). The attribution is to the New Kingdom or later. “Clean” and “unclean” as classes of animals occur elsewhere only in the Torah’s law codes (Lev 11; 20:25; 27:11, 27; Num 18:15; Deut 14). The closest outside parallels for restricting animals in the cult are Late Bronze temple rules (Hittite Instructions for Temple Officials, 14th–13th c.) and later Egyptian and Mesopotamian priestly and calendar restrictions. And the versions were still extending the scheme: the Septuagint adds clean and unclean birds at 7:3. Egypt’s pig taboo for Horus reaches back to the Middle Kingdom (Coffin Texts 157), so the later attribution rests chiefly on the Hebrew wording and the growth of the text.
  6. Later peoples, idioms, and links, tested blind. Two readers, working without the project’s hypotheses, coded sixteen remaining candidates as detachable, substitutable, or woven (agreement 19 of 21 items, controls included). The detachable ones are removed: Laban “the Aramean” (31:20, 24; Arameans are first attested c. 1100); “therefore his name was called Edom,” “the field of Edom,” and “Esau is Edom” (25:30; 32:4; 36:8); “because he had done folly in Israel” (34:7; the idiom recurs in 2 Sam 13:12); “the twelve tribes of Israel” (49:28); Jacob’s references to “the God of Abraham and the Fear of Isaac” and to “my fathers Abraham and Isaac” (31:42; 48:15–16), cross-cycle links of the joining phase; the Egyptian refusal to eat with Hebrews and the abomination of shepherds (43:32; 46:34); and “the God of heaven and the God of the earth” (24:3), a Persian-period formula. The substitutable ones stay, marked †: the oath by “the God of Abraham and the God of Nahor” and “the Fear of Isaac” (31:53), “the God of his father Isaac” at Beersheba (46:1), the name Asenath (41:45, 50; 46:20), whose type fits the first millennium best, and Joseph’s title “governor” (šallîṭ, 42:6), since read as “the lord of the land” (see “What was replaced”). Esau’s Priestly generations heading (36:9) is removed with the other headings.
  7. Canaan in Gen 9, and the Sodom story, tested blind. In Gen 9 Ham sees his father’s nakedness but Noah curses Canaan, and Noah knows what “his youngest son” had done (9:24), though Ham is the middle son. The older tale seems to have named Noah’s sons Shem, Japheth, and Canaan. Both readers coded “Ham is the father of Canaan” (9:18) and “the father of Canaan” (9:22) as detachable; they are removed as harmonizing glosses. The curse itself is woven and stays, marked †: Canaan as “a servant of servants” matches the Canaanite forced labor of Solomon’s reign (1 Kgs 9:20–21), an early-monarchy condition. Canaan is restored to Ham’s sons (10:6), marked †, so that the line in 10:15 has its parent. In the Sodom story both readers coded as detachable, and they are removed: Abraham’s bargaining over the righteous (18:23–32), a debate on collective and individual justice of the kind in Ezek 14 and 18; Lot’s wife and the pillar of salt (19:26), an etiology of the salt formations at Mount Sodom; the naming of Zoar (19:22b); and the aside comparing the plain to Eden and Egypt “before YHWH destroyed Sodom and Gomorrah” (13:10).
  8. Aramean and Priestly place labels, tested blind. Aram enters the record c. 1100 (Tiglath-pileser I), and Aramaic writing in the ninth century. Two readers coded eleven labels in the Jacob story, with calibration controls: four documented additions, two from the Chronicler’s reworking of Kings (2 Chr 8:11; 12:2) and two known glosses (28:19; 35:20), all four coded detachable by both; three of five woven controls coded woven, the misses being a theft whose consequence lies outside the verses shown (31:19) and a place name the readers called substitutable (37:17). Agreement was 20 of 22. Removed as detachable by both readers: Aram-naharaim (24:10, which already says “to the city of Nahor”); “in Paddan-aram” and “from Paddan-aram” (31:18; 33:18; 46:15; 48:7); “of Gilead” in the pursuit (31:23, 25, leaving “the mountain,” as 31:25 and 31:54 already say); and “in the land of Canaan” where it stands in verses Noth divides between P and the older story (31:18; 33:18; 48:7). The phrase “land of Canaan” is a Priestly preference: in Genesis it runs at 3.4 per 1,000 words in P against 1.1 in the older narrative (12 of 31 instances in P, which holds 18 percent of the words; binomial p = 0.007), it is rare in Exodus (2) and Deuteronomy (1), and in the older narrative 17 of its 19 instances stand in the Joseph story (Gen 37–50), where Canaan answers Egypt in the plot and stays. Kept and marked † where the readers split: Gilead at 31:21 and the magicians (41:8, 24).
  9. Court titles, chariots, camels, and two late names, tested blind. Blind set 4 put the remaining post-1550 innovations of the horizon audit before two readers, with the calibration controls of set 3 and one woven court title, the cupbearer of 40:1. Both readers coded all four documented additions detachable and the cupbearer woven; the theft of the teraphim (31:19), whose consequence lies outside the verses shown, and Dothan, which they called substitutable, missed again, as in set 3. Agreement was 23 of 27. Removed as detachable by both readers: Laban’s Aramaic name for the cairn, Jegar-sahadutha (31:47), in a language first written in the ninth century; “in the land of Shinar” (11:2); Joseph’s chariot at the reunion (46:29); the chariots and horsemen of the burial procession (50:9); and the camels in the servant’s list of his master’s wealth (24:35), as at 12:16. Kept and marked † where the readers coded the element woven or substitutable, or split: the camels of Gen 24, which both coded woven in the sign at the well (24:14, 19–20) and in the departure (24:10); the “second chariot” of Joseph’s investiture (41:43); the king’s birthday feast (40:20); and the captain of the guard. The readers coded that office substitutable where it acts (40:3–4) and detachable where it stands in apposition (37:36; 41:12), a pattern of apposition rather than of date, so the office is kept, marked †, at every mention. The camels are woven into the scene. Dromedary caravans reached the southern Levant only in the late tenth century (Lidar Sapir-Hen and Erez Ben-Yosef, “The Introduction of Domestic Camels to the Southern Levant: Evidence from the Aravah Valley,” Tel Aviv 40 [2013]: 277–85), which would date the chapter as now told. But the servant travels to the Harran country, where an Old Syrian cylinder seal of c. 1800 BCE shows two figures riding a two-humped Bactrian camel (Baltimore, Walters Art Museum 42.804) [verify catalog date], an animal of the eastern routes that carried lapis westward. Whether the story’s camels are Bactrians remembered from that road or a later teller’s dromedaries, the chapter does not say. Camels are cut where the readers coded them detachable (12:16; 24:35; 30:43; 32:8, 16) and kept with † where they carry the scene (every camel of Gen 24; 31:17, 34). The same applies to “the land of the Hebrews” and the chariots (removed at 46:29 and 50:9; kept † at 41:43). These camel decisions are equivocal. They are a compromise adopted for this experimental text, and they will stand until a better solution or a sharper test replaces them.
  10. Late Egyptian names. Potiphar, Potiphera, and Zaphenath-paneah. At 41:45 the sentence now reads that the king gave Joseph “an Egyptian name,” a paraphrase supplied here to keep the event.
  11. The southern addition. Gen 38, the Judah version of the sale (37:25–27), and the Ishmaelites who buy Joseph in 39:1. The sale now runs with Reuben and the Midianites alone.
  12. The Table of Nations, reduced to its first horizon. Gen 10 keeps only names attested before c. 1800 BCE: Cush and Mizraim; Nimrod’s Babel, Erech, Accad, and Nineveh in Asshur; Heth, the Amorites, and the Arkites; Elam and Asshur; and the line of Shem through Arpachshad to Eber, Peleg, and Joktan. Elam, Asshur, Cush, and Mizraim come back from the Priestly list (‡). Removed: the Nimrod saying (10:9), names first attested in the Late Bronze Age (Calah, the sons of Mizraim, Sidon and the later sons of Canaan), Joktan’s first-millennium Arabian sons, and the border note (10:19). Canaan stays, marked †, as the parent of Heth, the Amorites, and the Arkites.
  13. The genealogies of peoples, under the same rule. Four more lists name peoples rather than persons: Nahor’s twelve (22:20–24), Keturah’s sons (25:2–4), Ishmael’s twelve princes (25:13–16), and Esau’s sons and grandsons (36:2–5, 10–14). The rule that reduced the Table of Nations removes them. Every name in Ishmael’s list that can be placed belongs to the Arabian peoples of the Assyrian annals: Kedar, Adbeel, Massa, and Tema under Tiglath-pileser III, Dumah under Sennacherib, and probably Nebaioth under Ashurbanipal. Keturah’s Sheba, Dedan, and Ephah, Nahor’s Chesed (the Kaldu, from 878), Buz, and Hazo (Esarhaddon), and Kemuel’s son Aram (c. 1100) are first-millennium or late second-millennium names; Tebah and Tahash in Nahor’s line appear in the Amarna letters; Esau’s Teman first appears at Kuntillet ʿAjrud, c. 800. Shuah, sometimes equated with the Suḫûm of the Mari texts, is disputed. The persons the stories need stay: Milcah’s son Bethuel, the father of Rebekah (22:23, where “her son” is supplied from 24:15 and 24), and Keturah as Abraham’s wife (25:1), marked †: her name, “incense,” is unattested among Amorite personal names and belongs with the incense-road peoples of her sons’ first-millennium horizon.

What was kept, and what remains of the later phases

One name stays unmarked because it occurs too often to flag: “Canaan,” which is older than the Late Bronze Age. A Mari letter of the early eighteenth century BCE (A.3552; Georges Dossin, “Une mention de Cananéens dans une lettre de Mari,” Syria 50 [1973]) reports Kinaḫnū, “Canaanites,” and names them beside the ḫabbātum, “raiders,” facing the sender’s troops. The pairing suggests a group identity held in low esteem from its first appearance, a standing the curse of 9:25 casts as a servant’s lot. Whether the Mari Kinaḫnū are the Canaanites of the Levant is disputed (Anson F. Rainey, “Who Is a Canaanite?” BASOR 304 [1996]); the spelling, which keeps the final -n of Hebrew kənaʿan, favors it. In this text the people come first and the man Canaan second: no source outside the Bible and the Phoenician eponym Chna names an individual Canaan. The southern Abraham–Isaac tradition may have known the name YHWH on its own, since the Negev borders the Shasu lands of Seir and Edom; reading “God” there suits a first-phase text and does not claim the name is late everywhere. The division of the core persists in what the narrators do, not in the names: the Abraham and Isaac stories bring God bodily among people, the Jacob and Joseph stories keep God offstage. Jacob’s blessing (49:2–27) is kept as poetry, untested. Doublets stay in: three wife-sister stories, two expulsions of Hagar, two namings of Beersheba.

The Babel story (11:1–9) stays whole, beside the other primeval explanations: how clothing began (3:21), and wine (9:20), and the names of the animals (2:19–20). It accounts for the many languages of the earth and the scattering of peoples. No layer removed from this text does work of that kind; the later material is linking, tribal, cultic, and monarchical. The Priestly writer had an explanation of his own, in which the peoples spread out “by their languages” over the generations (10:5, 20, 31), and he set it beside the tower story without reconciling the two. The theme of a single human speech ended by a god has a Mesopotamian witness from the age of the family memory. In Enmerkar and the Lord of Aratta, a Sumerian epic composed under the Third Dynasty of Ur and copied in Old Babylonian schools, the “Spell of Nudimmud” recalls a time when all lands addressed Enlil “in one tongue,” and Enki changes the speech in their mouths (Samuel Noah Kramer, “The ‘Babel of Tongues’: A Sumerian Version,” Journal of the American Oriental Society 88 [1968]: 108–11). Herman Vanstiphout reads the passage as Enki uniting speech rather than dividing it (“Another Attempt at the ‘Spell of Nudimmud,’” Revue d’assyriologie 88 [1994]: 135–54 [verify pages]). Either way, the period knew a story of one language and a god who changed it. The pun on Babel and bālal, “confuse,” answers the Akkadian reading bāb-ili, “gate of god,” and works only in Hebrew; it shows the language of the telling, not the age of the story. Babylon is attested from the reign of Šar-kali-šarri of Akkad in the twenty-second century and became the leading city of the south under Hammurabi in the eighteenth. Christoph Uehlinger reads the unfinished city as Sargon II’s Dur-Šarrukin, abandoned after the king’s death in 705 BCE (Weltreich und “eine Rede”: Eine neue Deutung der sogenannten Turmbauerzählung (Gen 11,1–9), Orbis Biblicus et Orientalis 101 [Freiburg: Universitätsverlag; Göttingen: Vandenhoeck & Ruprecht, 1990]). An abandoned building project belongs to no single century, so his reading can date a retelling but not the story. Shinar (11:2), a Late Bronze name for Babylonia, is removed: both blind readers coded “in the land of Shinar” detachable (blind set 4), and the story names its city at 11:9.

The names of the core carry Hebrew explanations, a habit of the older, non-Priestly tellers; the Priestly writer names without explaining. An explanation needs only to sound right to its hearers, not to give the name’s origin. Babel, for example, is explained by bālal, “confuse” (11:9), while the city’s own name, Akkadian bāb-ili, means “gate of god.” The habit continues in Exodus. Moses is an Egyptian name of the type ms, “born,” as in Ahmose and Thutmose, yet it is explained as “I drew him out of the water” (Exod 2:10), though the Hebrew form mōšeh means “one who draws out.” In Genesis the same habit re-explains names of Amorite type: Jacob, in Amorite “may El protect,” becomes the twin who grasped his brother’s heel (25:26). The names are older than their explanations. None of the core’s wordplays works only in another language, but an explanation made in the hearers’ own language would erase any older pun, so this tells little about the language in which the stories were first told. The pattern may even point the other way: the stories may first have been told in an older language, and scribes after the first phase may have added the Hebrew explanations so that the names would be heard as Hebrew. If so, the explanations form a layer within the core that the tests here have not yet separated.

Sigla

‡
Priestly-stream content kept: legend, family law, or narrative needed for continuity, with its framework numbers removed
†
A later name or object kept because it fills a slot in the scene
§
Poetry, not tested
°
“Yahweh” of the base text read as “God”: the first phase predates the name
※
A later word of the base text read as the older word of the same story (“Pharaoh” as “the king”; the Philistines as Gerar; “governor” as “the lord of the land”; “officer” (sārîs) as “servant”; Bethel as Luz in Abraham’s journeys): the first phase predates the later word

Base text: World English Bible (public domain), renumbered to the Hebrew versification (Hebrew 32:1 = English 31:55). Removal rules: layer-map/layermap.py and josephite-text/build.py in the project.

I. Before the Fathers

11In the beginning, God created the heavens and the earth. 1בְּרֵאשִׁ֖ית בָּרָ֣א אֱלֹהִ֑ים אֵ֥ת הַשָּׁמַ֖יִם וְאֵ֥ת הָאָֽרֶץ׃2The earth was formless and empty. Darkness was on the surface of the deep and God’s Spirit was hovering over the surface of the waters. 2וְהָאָ֗רֶץ הָיְתָ֥ה תֹ֨הוּ֙ וָבֹ֔הוּ וְחֹ֖שֶׁךְ עַל־פְּנֵ֣י תְה֑וֹם וְר֣וּחַ אֱלֹהִ֔ים מְרַחֶ֖פֶת עַל־פְּנֵ֥י הַמָּֽיִם׃3God said, “Let there be light,” and there was light. 3וַיֹּ֥אמֶר אֱלֹהִ֖ים יְהִ֣י א֑וֹר וַֽיְהִי־אֽוֹר׃4God saw the light, and saw that it was good. God divided the light from the darkness. 4וַיַּ֧רְא אֱלֹהִ֛ים אֶת־הָא֖וֹר כִּי־ט֑וֹב וַיַּבְדֵּ֣ל אֱלֹהִ֔ים בֵּ֥ין הָא֖וֹר וּבֵ֥ין הַחֹֽשֶׁךְ׃5God called the light “day”, and the darkness he called “night”. There was evening and there was morning, the first day. 5וַיִּקְרָ֨א אֱלֹהִ֤ים ׀ לָאוֹר֙ י֔וֹם וְלַחֹ֖שֶׁךְ קָ֣רָא לָ֑יְלָה וַֽיְהִי־עֶ֥רֶב וַֽיְהִי־בֹ֖קֶר י֥וֹם אֶחָֽד׃ פ6God said, “Let there be an expanse in the middle of the waters, and let it divide the waters from the waters.” 6וַיֹּ֣אמֶר אֱלֹהִ֔ים יְהִ֥י רָקִ֖יעַ בְּת֣וֹךְ הַמָּ֑יִם וִיהִ֣י מַבְדִּ֔יל בֵּ֥ין מַ֖יִם לָמָֽיִם׃7God made the expanse, and divided the waters which were under the expanse from the waters which were above the expanse; and it was so. 7וַיַּ֣עַשׂ אֱלֹהִים֮ אֶת־הָרָקִיעַ֒ וַיַּבְדֵּ֗ל בֵּ֤ין הַמַּ֨יִם֙ אֲשֶׁר֙ מִתַּ֣חַת לָרָקִ֔יעַ וּבֵ֣ין הַמַּ֔יִם אֲשֶׁ֖ר מֵעַ֣ל לָרָקִ֑יעַ וַֽיְהִי־כֵֽן׃8God called the expanse “sky”. There was evening and there was morning, a second day. 8וַיִּקְרָ֧א אֱלֹהִ֛ים לָֽרָקִ֖יעַ שָׁמָ֑יִם וַֽיְהִי־עֶ֥רֶב וַֽיְהִי־בֹ֖קֶר י֥וֹם שֵׁנִֽי׃ פ9God said, “Let the waters under the sky be gathered together to one place, and let the dry land appear”; and it was so. 9וַיֹּ֣אמֶר אֱלֹהִ֗ים יִקָּו֨וּ הַמַּ֜יִם מִתַּ֤חַת הַשָּׁמַ֨יִם֙ אֶל־מָק֣וֹם אֶחָ֔ד וְתֵרָאֶ֖ה הַיַּבָּשָׁ֑ה וַֽיְהִי־כֵֽן׃10God called the dry land “earth”, and the gathering together of the waters he called “seas”. God saw that it was good. 10וַיִּקְרָ֨א אֱלֹהִ֤ים ׀ לַיַּבָּשָׁה֙ אֶ֔רֶץ וּלְמִקְוֵ֥ה הַמַּ֖יִם קָרָ֣א יַמִּ֑ים וַיַּ֥רְא אֱלֹהִ֖ים כִּי־טֽוֹב׃11God said, “Let the earth yield grass, herbs yielding seeds, and fruit trees bearing fruit after their kind, with their seeds in it, on the earth”; and it was so. 11וַיֹּ֣אמֶר אֱלֹהִ֗ים תַּֽדְשֵׁ֤א הָאָ֨רֶץ֙ דֶּ֔שֶׁא עֵ֚שֶׂב מַזְרִ֣יעַ זֶ֔רַע עֵ֣ץ פְּרִ֞י עֹ֤שֶׂה פְּרִי֙ לְמִינ֔וֹ אֲשֶׁ֥ר זַרְעוֹ־ב֖וֹ עַל־הָאָ֑רֶץ וַֽיְהִי־כֵֽן׃12The earth yielded grass, herbs yielding seed after their kind, and trees bearing fruit, with their seeds in it, after their kind; and God saw that it was good. 12וַתּוֹצֵ֨א הָאָ֜רֶץ דֶּ֠שֶׁא עֵ֣שֶׂב מַזְרִ֤יעַ זֶ֨רַע֙ לְמִינֵ֔הוּ וְעֵ֧ץ עֹֽשֶׂה־פְּרִ֛י אֲשֶׁ֥ר זַרְעוֹ־ב֖וֹ לְמִינֵ֑הוּ וַיַּ֥רְא אֱלֹהִ֖ים כִּי־טֽוֹב׃13There was evening and there was morning, a third day. 13וַֽיְהִי־עֶ֥רֶב וַֽיְהִי־בֹ֖קֶר י֥וֹם שְׁלִישִֽׁי׃ פ14God said, “Let there be lights in the expanse of the sky to divide the day from the night; and let them be for signs to mark seasons, days, and years; 14וַיֹּ֣אמֶר אֱלֹהִ֗ים יְהִ֤י מְאֹרֹת֙ בִּרְקִ֣יעַ הַשָּׁמַ֔יִם לְהַבְדִּ֕יל בֵּ֥ין הַיּ֖וֹם וּבֵ֣ין הַלָּ֑יְלָה וְהָי֤וּ לְאֹתֹת֙ וּלְמ֣וֹעֲדִ֔ים וּלְיָמִ֖ים וְשָׁנִֽים׃15and let them be for lights in the expanse of the sky to give light on the earth”; and it was so. 15וְהָי֤וּ לִמְאוֹרֹת֙ בִּרְקִ֣יעַ הַשָּׁמַ֔יִם לְהָאִ֖יר עַל־הָאָ֑רֶץ וַֽיְהִי־כֵֽן׃16God made the two great lights: the greater light to rule the day, and the lesser light to rule the night. He also made the stars. 16וַיַּ֣עַשׂ אֱלֹהִ֔ים אֶת־שְׁנֵ֥י הַמְּאֹרֹ֖ת הַגְּדֹלִ֑ים אֶת־הַמָּא֤וֹר הַגָּדֹל֙ לְמֶמְשֶׁ֣לֶת הַיּ֔וֹם וְאֶת־הַמָּא֤וֹר הַקָּטֹן֙ לְמֶמְשֶׁ֣לֶת הַלַּ֔יְלָה וְאֵ֖ת הַכּוֹכָבִֽים׃17God set them in the expanse of the sky to give light to the earth, 17וַיִּתֵּ֥ן אֹתָ֛ם אֱלֹהִ֖ים בִּרְקִ֣יעַ הַשָּׁמָ֑יִם לְהָאִ֖יר עַל־הָאָֽרֶץ׃18and to rule over the day and over the night, and to divide the light from the darkness. God saw that it was good. 18וְלִמְשֹׁל֙ בַּיּ֣וֹם וּבַלַּ֔יְלָה וּֽלֲהַבְדִּ֔יל בֵּ֥ין הָא֖וֹר וּבֵ֣ין הַחֹ֑שֶׁךְ וַיַּ֥רְא אֱלֹהִ֖ים כִּי־טֽוֹב׃19There was evening and there was morning, a fourth day. 19וַֽיְהִי־עֶ֥רֶב וַֽיְהִי־בֹ֖קֶר י֥וֹם רְבִיעִֽי׃ פ20God said, “Let the waters abound with living creatures, and let birds fly above the earth in the open expanse of the sky.” 20וַיֹּ֣אמֶר אֱלֹהִ֔ים יִשְׁרְצ֣וּ הַמַּ֔יִם שֶׁ֖רֶץ נֶ֣פֶשׁ חַיָּ֑ה וְעוֹף֙ יְעוֹפֵ֣ף עַל־הָאָ֔רֶץ עַל־פְּנֵ֖י רְקִ֥יעַ הַשָּׁמָֽיִם׃21God created the large sea creatures and every living creature that moves, with which the waters swarmed, after their kind, and every winged bird after its kind. God saw that it was good. 21וַיִּבְרָ֣א אֱלֹהִ֔ים אֶת־הַתַּנִּינִ֖ם הַגְּדֹלִ֑ים וְאֵ֣ת כָּל־נֶ֣פֶשׁ הַֽחַיָּ֣ה ׀ הָֽרֹמֶ֡שֶׂת אֲשֶׁר֩ שָׁרְצ֨וּ הַמַּ֜יִם לְמִֽינֵהֶ֗ם וְאֵ֨ת כָּל־ע֤וֹף כָּנָף֙ לְמִינֵ֔הוּ וַיַּ֥רְא אֱלֹהִ֖ים כִּי־טֽוֹב׃22God blessed them, saying, “Be fruitful, and multiply, and fill the waters in the seas, and let birds multiply on the earth.” 22וַיְבָ֧רֶךְ אֹתָ֛ם אֱלֹהִ֖ים לֵאמֹ֑ר פְּר֣וּ וּרְב֗וּ וּמִלְא֤וּ אֶת־הַמַּ֨יִם֙ בַּיַּמִּ֔ים וְהָע֖וֹף יִ֥רֶב בָּאָֽרֶץ׃23There was evening and there was morning, a fifth day. 23וַֽיְהִי־עֶ֥רֶב וַֽיְהִי־בֹ֖קֶר י֥וֹם חֲמִישִֽׁי׃ פ24God said, “Let the earth produce living creatures after their kind, livestock, creeping things, and animals of the earth after their kind”; and it was so. 24וַיֹּ֣אמֶר אֱלֹהִ֗ים תּוֹצֵ֨א הָאָ֜רֶץ נֶ֤פֶשׁ חַיָּה֙ לְמִינָ֔הּ בְּהֵמָ֥ה וָרֶ֛מֶשׂ וְחַֽיְתוֹ־אֶ֖רֶץ לְמִינָ֑הּ וַֽיְהִי־כֵֽן׃25God made the animals of the earth after their kind, and the livestock after their kind, and everything that creeps on the ground after its kind. God saw that it was good. 25וַיַּ֣עַשׂ אֱלֹהִים֩ אֶת־חַיַּ֨ת הָאָ֜רֶץ לְמִינָ֗הּ וְאֶת־הַבְּהֵמָה֙ לְמִינָ֔הּ וְאֵ֛ת כָּל־רֶ֥מֶשׂ הָֽאֲדָמָ֖ה לְמִינֵ֑הוּ וַיַּ֥רְא אֱלֹהִ֖ים כִּי־טֽוֹב׃26God said, “Let us make man in our image, after our likeness: and let them have dominion over the fish of the sea, and over the birds of the sky, and over the livestock, and over all the earth, and over every creeping thing that creeps on the earth.” 26וַיֹּ֣אמֶר אֱלֹהִ֔ים נַֽעֲשֶׂ֥ה אָדָ֛ם בְּצַלְמֵ֖נוּ כִּדְמוּתֵ֑נוּ וְיִרְדּוּ֩ בִדְגַ֨ת הַיָּ֜ם וּבְע֣וֹף הַשָּׁמַ֗יִם וּבַבְּהֵמָה֙ וּבְכָל־הָאָ֔רֶץ וּבְכָל־הָרֶ֖מֶשׂ הָֽרֹמֵ֥שׂ עַל־הָאָֽרֶץ׃27God created man in his own image. In God’s image he created him; male and female he created them. 27וַיִּבְרָ֨א אֱלֹהִ֤ים ׀ אֶת־הָֽאָדָם֙ בְּצַלְמ֔וֹ בְּצֶ֥לֶם אֱלֹהִ֖ים בָּרָ֣א אֹת֑וֹ זָכָ֥ר וּנְקֵבָ֖ה בָּרָ֥א אֹתָֽם׃28God blessed them. God said to them, “Be fruitful, multiply, fill the earth, and subdue it. Have dominion over the fish of the sea, over the birds of the sky, and over every living thing that moves on the earth.” 28וַיְבָ֣רֶךְ אֹתָם֮ אֱלֹהִים֒ וַיֹּ֨אמֶר לָהֶ֜ם אֱלֹהִ֗ים פְּר֥וּ וּרְב֛וּ וּמִלְא֥וּ אֶת־הָאָ֖רֶץ וְכִבְשֻׁ֑הָ וּרְד֞וּ בִּדְגַ֤ת הַיָּם֙ וּבְע֣וֹף הַשָּׁמַ֔יִם וּבְכָל־חַיָּ֖ה הָֽרֹמֶ֥שֶׂת עַל־הָאָֽרֶץ׃29God said, “Behold, I have given you every herb yielding seed, which is on the surface of all the earth, and every tree, which bears fruit yielding seed. It will be your food. 29וַיֹּ֣אמֶר אֱלֹהִ֗ים הִנֵּה֩ נָתַ֨תִּי לָכֶ֜ם אֶת־כָּל־עֵ֣שֶׂב ׀ זֹרֵ֣עַ זֶ֗רַע אֲשֶׁר֙ עַל־פְּנֵ֣י כָל־הָאָ֔רֶץ וְאֶת־כָּל־הָעֵ֛ץ אֲשֶׁר־בּ֥וֹ פְרִי־עֵ֖ץ זֹרֵ֣עַ זָ֑רַע לָכֶ֥ם יִֽהְיֶ֖ה לְאָכְלָֽה׃30To every animal of the earth, and to every bird of the sky, and to everything that creeps on the earth, in which there is life, I have given every green herb for food;” and it was so. 30וּֽלְכָל־חַיַּ֣ת הָ֠אָרֶץ וּלְכָל־ע֨וֹף הַשָּׁמַ֜יִם וּלְכֹ֣ל ׀ רוֹמֵ֣שׂ עַל־הָאָ֗רֶץ אֲשֶׁר־בּוֹ֙ נֶ֣פֶשׁ חַיָּ֔ה אֶת־כָּל־יֶ֥רֶק עֵ֖שֶׂב לְאָכְלָ֑ה וַֽיְהִי־כֵֽן׃31God saw everything that he had made, and, behold, it was very good. There was evening and there was morning, a sixth day. 31וַיַּ֤רְא אֱלֹהִים֙ אֶת־כָּל־אֲשֶׁ֣ר עָשָׂ֔ה וְהִנֵּה־ט֖וֹב מְאֹ֑ד וַֽיְהִי־עֶ֥רֶב וַֽיְהִי־בֹ֖קֶר י֥וֹם הַשִּׁשִּֽׁי׃ פ

21The heavens, the earth, and all their vast array were finished. 1וַיְכֻלּ֛וּ הַשָּׁמַ֥יִם וְהָאָ֖רֶץ וְכָל־צְבָאָֽם׃2On the seventh day God finished his work which he had done; and he rested on the seventh day from all his work which he had done. 2וַיְכַ֤ל אֱלֹהִים֙ בַּיּ֣וֹם הַשְּׁבִיעִ֔י מְלַאכְתּ֖וֹ אֲשֶׁ֣ר עָשָׂ֑ה וַיִּשְׁבֹּת֙ בַּיּ֣וֹם הַשְּׁבִיעִ֔י מִכָּל־מְלַאכְתּ֖וֹ אֲשֶׁ֥ר עָשָֽׂה׃3God blessed the seventh day, and made it holy, because he rested in it from all his work of creation which he had done. 3וַיְבָ֤רֶךְ אֱלֹהִים֙ אֶת־י֣וֹם הַשְּׁבִיעִ֔י וַיְקַדֵּ֖שׁ אֹת֑וֹ כִּ֣י ב֤וֹ שָׁבַת֙ מִכָּל־מְלַאכְתּ֔וֹ אֲשֶׁר־בָּרָ֥א אֱלֹהִ֖ים לַעֲשֽׂוֹת׃ פ4In the day that God made the earth and the heavens, 4אֵ֣לֶּה תוֹלְד֧וֹת הַשָּׁמַ֛יִם וְהָאָ֖רֶץ בְּהִבָּֽרְאָ֑ם בְּי֗וֹם עֲשׂ֛וֹת יְהוָ֥ה אֱלֹהִ֖ים אֶ֥רֶץ וְשָׁמָֽיִם׃5no plant of the field was yet in the earth, and no herb of the field had yet sprung up; for God had not caused it to rain on the earth. There was not a man to till the ground, 5וְכֹ֣ל ׀ שִׂ֣יחַ הַשָּׂדֶ֗ה טֶ֚רֶם יִֽהְיֶ֣ה בָאָ֔רֶץ וְכָל־עֵ֥שֶׂב הַשָּׂדֶ֖ה טֶ֣רֶם יִצְמָ֑ח כִּי֩ לֹ֨א הִמְטִ֜יר יְהוָ֤ה אֱלֹהִים֙ עַל־הָאָ֔רֶץ וְאָדָ֣ם אַ֔יִן לַֽעֲבֹ֖ד אֶת־הָֽאֲדָמָֽה׃6but a mist went up from the earth, and watered the whole surface of the ground. 6וְאֵ֖ד יַֽעֲלֶ֣ה מִן־הָאָ֑רֶץ וְהִשְׁקָ֖ה אֶֽת־כָּל־פְּנֵֽי־הָֽאֲדָמָֽה׃7God formed man from the dust of the ground, and breathed into his nostrils the breath of life; and man became a living soul. 7וַיִּיצֶר֩ יְהוָ֨ה אֱלֹהִ֜ים אֶת־הָֽאָדָ֗ם עָפָר֙ מִן־הָ֣אֲדָמָ֔ה וַיִּפַּ֥ח בְּאַפָּ֖יו נִשְׁמַ֣ת חַיִּ֑ים וַֽיְהִ֥י הָֽאָדָ֖ם לְנֶ֥פֶשׁ חַיָּֽה׃8God planted a garden eastward, in Eden, and there he put the man whom he had formed. 8וַיִּטַּ֞ע יְהוָ֧ה אֱלֹהִ֛ים גַּן־בְעֵ֖דֶן מִקֶּ֑דֶם וַיָּ֣שֶׂם שָׁ֔ם אֶת־הָֽאָדָ֖ם אֲשֶׁ֥ר יָצָֽר׃9Out of the ground God made every tree to grow that is pleasant to the sight, and good for food, including the tree of life in the middle of the garden and the tree of the knowledge of good and evil. 9וַיַּצְמַ֞ח יְהוָ֤ה אֱלֹהִים֙ מִן־הָ֣אֲדָמָ֔ה כָּל־עֵ֛ץ נֶחְמָ֥ד לְמַרְאֶ֖ה וְט֣וֹב לְמַאֲכָ֑ל וְעֵ֤ץ הַֽחַיִּים֙ בְּת֣וֹךְ הַגָּ֔ן וְעֵ֕ץ הַדַּ֖עַת ט֥וֹב וָרָֽע׃10A river went out of Eden to water the garden; and from there it was parted, and became the source of four rivers. 10וְנָהָרּ֙ יֹצֵ֣א מֵעֵ֔דֶן לְהַשְׁק֖וֹת אֶת־הַגָּ֑ן וּמִשָּׁם֙ יִפָּרֵ֔ד וְהָיָ֖ה לְאַרְבָּעָ֥ה רָאשִֽׁים׃11The name of the first is Pishon: it flows through the whole land of Havilah, where there is gold; 11שֵׁ֥ם הָֽאֶחָ֖ד פִּישׁ֑וֹן ה֣וּא הַסֹּבֵ֗ב אֵ֚ת כָּל־אֶ֣רֶץ הַֽחֲוִילָ֔ה אֲשֶׁר־שָׁ֖ם הַזָּהָֽב׃12and the gold of that land is good. Bdellium and onyx stone are also there. 12וּֽזֲהַ֛ב הָאָ֥רֶץ הַהִ֖וא ט֑וֹב שָׁ֥ם הַבְּדֹ֖לַח וְאֶ֥בֶן הַשֹּֽׁהַם׃13The name of the second river is Gihon. It is the same river that flows through the whole land of Cush. 13וְשֵֽׁם־הַנָּהָ֥ר הַשֵּׁנִ֖י גִּיח֑וֹן ה֣וּא הַסּוֹבֵ֔ב אֵ֖ת כָּל־אֶ֥רֶץ כּֽוּשׁ׃14The name of the third river is Hiddekel. This is the one which flows in front of Assyria. The fourth river is the Euphrates. 14וְשֵׁ֨ם הַנָּהָ֤ר הַשְּׁלִישִׁי֙ חִדֶּ֔קֶל ה֥וּא הַֽהֹלֵ֖ךְ קִדְמַ֣ת אַשּׁ֑וּר וְהַנָּהָ֥ר הָֽרְבִיעִ֖י ה֥וּא פְרָֽת׃15God took the man, and put him into the garden of Eden to cultivate and keep it. 15וַיִּקַּ֛ח יְהוָ֥ה אֱלֹהִ֖ים אֶת־הָֽאָדָ֑ם וַיַּנִּחֵ֣הוּ בְגַן־עֵ֔דֶן לְעָבְדָ֖הּ וּלְשָׁמְרָֽהּ׃16God commanded the man, saying, “You may freely eat of every tree of the garden; 16וַיְצַו֙ יְהוָ֣ה אֱלֹהִ֔ים עַל־הָֽאָדָ֖ם לֵאמֹ֑ר מִכֹּ֥ל עֵֽץ־הַגָּ֖ן אָכֹ֥ל תֹּאכֵֽל׃17but you shall not eat of the tree of the knowledge of good and evil; for in the day that you eat of it, you will surely die.” 17וּמֵעֵ֗ץ הַדַּ֨עַת֙ ט֣וֹב וָרָ֔ע לֹ֥א תֹאכַ֖ל מִמֶּ֑נּוּ כִּ֗י בְּי֛וֹם אֲכָלְךָ֥ מִמֶּ֖נּוּ מ֥וֹת תָּמֽוּת׃18God said, “It is not good for the man to be alone. I will make him a helper comparable to him.” 18וַיֹּ֨אמֶר֙ יְהוָ֣ה אֱלֹהִ֔ים לֹא־ט֛וֹב הֱי֥וֹת הָֽאָדָ֖ם לְבַדּ֑וֹ אֶֽעֱשֶׂהּ־לּ֥וֹ עֵ֖זֶר כְּנֶגְדּֽוֹ׃19Out of the ground God formed every animal of the field, and every bird of the sky, and brought them to the man to see what he would call them. Whatever the man called every living creature became its name. 19וַיִּצֶר֩ יְהוָ֨ה אֱלֹהִ֜ים מִן־הָֽאֲדָמָ֗ה כָּל־חַיַּ֤ת הַשָּׂדֶה֙ וְאֵת֙ כָּל־ע֣וֹף הַשָּׁמַ֔יִם וַיָּבֵא֙ אֶל־הָ֣אָדָ֔ם לִרְא֖וֹת מַה־יִּקְרָא־ל֑וֹ וְכֹל֩ אֲשֶׁ֨ר יִקְרָא־ל֧וֹ הָֽאָדָ֛ם נֶ֥פֶשׁ חַיָּ֖ה ה֥וּא שְׁמֽוֹ׃20The man gave names to all livestock, and to the birds of the sky, and to every animal of the field; but for man there was not found a helper comparable to him. 20וַיִּקְרָ֨א הָֽאָדָ֜ם שֵׁמ֗וֹת לְכָל־הַבְּהֵמָה֙ וּלְע֣וֹף הַשָּׁמַ֔יִם וּלְכֹ֖ל חַיַּ֣ת הַשָּׂדֶ֑ה וּלְאָדָ֕ם לֹֽא־מָצָ֥א עֵ֖זֶר כְּנֶגְדּֽוֹ׃21God caused the man to fall into a deep sleep. As the man slept, he took one of his ribs, and closed up the flesh in its place. 21וַיַּפֵּל֩ יְהוָ֨ה אֱלֹהִ֧ים ׀ תַּרְדֵּמָ֛ה עַל־הָאָדָ֖ם וַיִּישָׁ֑ן וַיִּקַּ֗ח אַחַת֙ מִצַּלְעֹתָ֔יו וַיִּסְגֹּ֥ר בָּשָׂ֖ר תַּחְתֶּֽנָּה׃22God made a woman from the rib which he had taken from the man, and brought her to the man. 22וַיִּבֶן֩ יְהוָ֨ה אֱלֹהִ֧ים ׀ אֶֽת־הַצֵּלָ֛ע אֲשֶׁר־לָקַ֥ח מִן־הָֽאָדָ֖ם לְאִשָּׁ֑ה וַיְבִאֶ֖הָ אֶל־הָֽאָדָֽם׃23The man said, “This is now bone of my bones, and flesh of my flesh. She will be called ‘woman,’ because she was taken out of Man.” 23וַיֹּאמֶר֮ הָֽאָדָם֒ זֹ֣את הַפַּ֗עַם עֶ֚צֶם מֵֽעֲצָמַ֔י וּבָשָׂ֖ר מִבְּשָׂרִ֑י לְזֹאת֙ יִקָּרֵ֣א אִשָּׁ֔ה כִּ֥י מֵאִ֖ישׁ לֻֽקֳחָה־זֹּֽאת׃24Therefore a man will leave his father and his mother, and will join with his wife, and they will be one flesh. 24עַל־כֵּן֙ יַֽעֲזָב־אִ֔ישׁ אֶת־אָבִ֖יו וְאֶת־אִמּ֑וֹ וְדָבַ֣ק בְּאִשְׁתּ֔וֹ וְהָי֖וּ לְבָשָׂ֥ר אֶחָֽד׃25The man and his wife were both naked, and they were not ashamed. 25וַיִּֽהְי֤וּ שְׁנֵיהֶם֙ עֲרוּמִּ֔ים הָֽאָדָ֖ם וְאִשְׁתּ֑וֹ וְלֹ֖א יִתְבֹּשָֽׁשׁוּ׃

31Now the serpent was more subtle than any animal of the field which God had made. He said to the woman, “Has God really said, ‘You shall not eat of any tree of the garden?’” 1וְהַנָּחָשׁ֙ הָיָ֣ה עָר֔וּם מִכֹּל֙ חַיַּ֣ת הַשָּׂדֶ֔ה אֲשֶׁ֥ר עָשָׂ֖ה יְהוָ֣ה אֱלֹהִ֑ים וַיֹּ֨אמֶר֙ אֶל־הָ֣אִשָּׁ֔ה אַ֚ף כִּֽי־אָמַ֣ר אֱלֹהִ֔ים לֹ֣א תֹֽאכְל֔וּ מִכֹּ֖ל עֵ֥ץ הַגָּֽן׃2The woman said to the serpent, “We may eat fruit from the trees of the garden, 2וַתֹּ֥אמֶר הָֽאִשָּׁ֖ה אֶל־הַנָּחָ֑שׁ מִפְּרִ֥י עֵֽץ־הַגָּ֖ן נֹאכֵֽל׃3but not the fruit of the tree which is in the middle of the garden. God has said, ‘You shall not eat of it. You shall not touch it, lest you die.’” 3וּמִפְּרִ֣י הָעֵץ֮ אֲשֶׁ֣ר בְּתוֹךְ־הַגָּן֒ אָמַ֣ר אֱלֹהִ֗ים לֹ֤א תֹֽאכְלוּ֙ מִמֶּ֔נּוּ וְלֹ֥א תִגְּע֖וּ בּ֑וֹ פֶּן־תְּמֻתֽוּן׃4The serpent said to the woman, “You won’t really die, 4וַיֹּ֥אמֶר הַנָּחָ֖שׁ אֶל־הָֽאִשָּׁ֑ה לֹֽא־מ֖וֹת תְּמֻתֽוּן׃5for God knows that in the day you eat it, your eyes will be opened, and you will be like God, knowing good and evil.” 5כִּ֚י יֹדֵ֣עַ אֱלֹהִ֔ים כִּ֗י בְּיוֹם֙ אֲכָלְכֶ֣ם מִמֶּ֔נּוּ וְנִפְקְח֖וּ עֵֽינֵיכֶ֑ם וִהְיִיתֶם֙ כֵּֽאלֹהִ֔ים יֹדְעֵ֖י ט֥וֹב וָרָֽע׃6When the woman saw that the tree was good for food, and that it was a delight to the eyes, and that the tree was to be desired to make one wise, she took some of its fruit, and ate; and she gave some to her husband with her, and he ate it, too. 6וַתֵּ֣רֶא הָֽאִשָּׁ֡ה כִּ֣י טוֹב֩ הָעֵ֨ץ לְמַאֲכָ֜ל וְכִ֧י תַֽאֲוָה־ה֣וּא לָעֵינַ֗יִם וְנֶחְמָ֤ד הָעֵץ֙ לְהַשְׂכִּ֔יל וַתִּקַּ֥ח מִפִּרְי֖וֹ וַתֹּאכַ֑ל וַתִּתֵּ֧ן גַּם־לְאִישָׁ֛הּ עִמָּ֖הּ וַיֹּאכַֽל׃7Their eyes were opened, and they both knew that they were naked. They sewed fig leaves together, and made coverings for themselves. 7וַתִּפָּקַ֨חְנָה֙ עֵינֵ֣י שְׁנֵיהֶ֔ם וַיֵּ֣דְע֔וּ כִּ֥י עֵֽירֻמִּ֖ם הֵ֑ם וַֽיִּתְפְּרוּ֙ עֲלֵ֣ה תְאֵנָ֔ה וַיַּעֲשׂ֥וּ לָהֶ֖ם חֲגֹרֹֽת׃8They heard God’s voice walking in the garden in the cool of the day, and the man and his wife hid themselves from the presence of God among the trees of the garden. 8וַֽיִּשְׁמְע֞וּ אֶת־ק֨וֹל יְהוָ֧ה אֱלֹהִ֛ים מִתְהַלֵּ֥ךְ בַּגָּ֖ן לְר֣וּחַ הַיּ֑וֹם וַיִּתְחַבֵּ֨א הָֽאָדָ֜ם וְאִשְׁתּ֗וֹ מִפְּנֵי֙ יְהוָ֣ה אֱלֹהִ֔ים בְּת֖וֹךְ עֵ֥ץ הַגָּֽן׃9God called to the man, and said to him, “Where are you?” 9וַיִּקְרָ֛א יְהוָ֥ה אֱלֹהִ֖ים אֶל־הָֽאָדָ֑ם וַיֹּ֥אמֶר ל֖וֹ אַיֶּֽכָּה׃10The man said, “I heard your voice in the garden, and I was afraid, because I was naked; and I hid myself.” 10וַיֹּ֕אמֶר אֶת־קֹלְךָ֥ שָׁמַ֖עְתִּי בַּגָּ֑ן וָאִירָ֛א כִּֽי־עֵירֹ֥ם אָנֹ֖כִי וָאֵחָבֵֽא׃11God said, “Who told you that you were naked? Have you eaten from the tree that I commanded you not to eat from?” 11וַיֹּ֕אמֶר מִ֚י הִגִּ֣יד לְךָ֔ כִּ֥י עֵירֹ֖ם אָ֑תָּה הֲמִן־הָעֵ֗ץ אֲשֶׁ֧ר צִוִּיתִ֛יךָ לְבִלְתִּ֥י אֲכָל־מִמֶּ֖נּוּ אָכָֽלְתָּ׃12The man said, “The woman whom you gave to be with me, she gave me fruit from the tree, and I ate it.” 12וַיֹּ֖אמֶר הָֽאָדָ֑ם הָֽאִשָּׁה֙ אֲשֶׁ֣ר נָתַ֣תָּה עִמָּדִ֔י הִ֛וא נָֽתְנָה־לִּ֥י מִן־הָעֵ֖ץ וָאֹכֵֽל׃13God said to the woman, “What have you done?” The woman said, “The serpent deceived me, and I ate.” 13וַיֹּ֨אמֶר יְהוָ֧ה אֱלֹהִ֛ים לָאִשָּׁ֖ה מַה־זֹּ֣את עָשִׂ֑ית וַתֹּ֨אמֶר֙ הָֽאִשָּׁ֔ה הַנָּחָ֥שׁ הִשִּׁיאַ֖נִי וָאֹכֵֽל׃14God said to the serpent, “Because you have done this, you are cursed above all livestock, and above every animal of the field. You shall go on your belly and you shall eat dust all the days of your life. 14וַיֹּאמֶר֩ יְהֹוָ֨ה אֱלֹהִ֥ים ׀ אֶֽל־הַנָּחָשׁ֮ כִּ֣י עָשִׂ֣יתָ זֹּאת֒ אָר֤וּר אַתָּה֙ מִכָּל־הַבְּהֵמָ֔ה וּמִכֹּ֖ל חַיַּ֣ת הַשָּׂדֶ֑ה עַל־גְּחֹנְךָ֣ תֵלֵ֔ךְ וְעָפָ֥ר תֹּאכַ֖ל כָּל־יְמֵ֥י חַיֶּֽיךָ׃15I will put hostility between you and the woman, and between your offspring and her offspring. He will bruise your head, and you will bruise his heel.” 15וְאֵיבָ֣ה ׀ אָשִׁ֗ית בֵּֽינְךָ֙ וּבֵ֣ין הָֽאִשָּׁ֔ה וּבֵ֥ין זַרְעֲךָ֖ וּבֵ֣ין זַרְעָ֑הּ ה֚וּא יְשׁוּפְךָ֣ רֹ֔אשׁ וְאַתָּ֖ה תְּשׁוּפֶ֥נּוּ עָקֵֽב׃ ס16To the woman he said, “I will greatly multiply your pain in childbirth. In pain you will bear children. Your desire will be for your husband, and he will rule over you.” 16אֶֽל־הָאִשָּׁ֣ה אָמַ֗ר הַרְבָּ֤ה אַרְבֶּה֙ עִצְּבוֹנֵ֣ךְ וְהֵֽרֹנֵ֔ךְ בְּעֶ֖צֶב תֵּֽלְדִ֣י בָנִ֑ים וְאֶל־אִישֵׁךְ֙ תְּשׁ֣וּקָתֵ֔ךְ וְה֖וּא יִמְשָׁל־בָּֽךְ׃ ס17To Adam he said, “Because you have listened to your wife’s voice, and ate from the tree, about which I commanded you, saying, ‘You shall not eat of it,’ the ground is cursed for your sake. You will eat from it with much labor all the days of your life. 17וּלְאָדָ֣ם אָמַ֗ר כִּֽי־שָׁמַעְתָּ֮ לְק֣וֹל אִשְׁתֶּךָ֒ וַתֹּ֨אכַל֙ מִן־הָעֵ֔ץ אֲשֶׁ֤ר צִוִּיתִ֨יךָ֙ לֵאמֹ֔ר לֹ֥א תֹאכַ֖ל מִמֶּ֑נּוּ אֲרוּרָ֤ה הָֽאֲדָמָה֙ בַּֽעֲבוּרֶ֔ךָ בְּעִצָּבוֹן֙ תֹּֽאכֲלֶ֔נָּה כֹּ֖ל יְמֵ֥י חַיֶּֽיךָ׃18It will yield thorns and thistles to you; and you will eat the herb of the field. 18וְק֥וֹץ וְדַרְדַּ֖ר תַּצְמִ֣יחַֽ לָ֑ךְ וְאָכַלְתָּ֖ אֶת־עֵ֥שֶׂב הַשָּׂדֶֽה׃19By the sweat of your face will you eat bread until you return to the ground, for out of it you were taken. For you are dust, and to dust you shall return.” 19בְּזֵעַ֤ת אַפֶּ֨יךָ֙ תֹּ֣אכַל לֶ֔חֶם עַ֤ד שֽׁוּבְךָ֙ אֶל־הָ֣אֲדָמָ֔ה כִּ֥י מִמֶּ֖נָּה לֻקָּ֑חְתָּ כִּֽי־עָפָ֣ר אַ֔תָּה וְאֶל־עָפָ֖ר תָּשֽׁוּב׃20The man called his wife Eve because she would be the mother of all the living. 20וַיִּקְרָ֧א הָֽאָדָ֛ם שֵׁ֥ם אִשְׁתּ֖וֹ חַוָּ֑ה כִּ֛י הִ֥וא הָֽיְתָ֖ה אֵ֥ם כָּל־חָֽי׃21God made coats of animal skins for Adam and for his wife, and clothed them. 21וַיַּעַשׂ֩ יְהוָ֨ה אֱלֹהִ֜ים לְאָדָ֧ם וּלְאִשְׁתּ֛וֹ כָּתְנ֥וֹת ע֖וֹר וַיַּלְבִּשֵֽׁם׃ פ22God said, “Behold, the man has become like one of us, knowing good and evil. Now, lest he reach out his hand, and also take of the tree of life, and eat, and live forever—” 22וַיֹּ֣אמֶר ׀ יְהוָ֣ה אֱלֹהִ֗ים הֵ֤ן הָֽאָדָם֙ הָיָה֙ כְּאַחַ֣ד מִמֶּ֔נּוּ לָדַ֖עַת ט֣וֹב וָרָ֑ע וְעַתָּ֣ה ׀ פֶּן־יִשְׁלַ֣ח יָד֗וֹ וְלָקַח֙ גַּ֚ם מֵעֵ֣ץ הַֽחַיִּ֔ים וְאָכַ֖ל וָחַ֥י לְעֹלָֽם׃23Therefore God sent him out from the garden of Eden, to till the ground from which he was taken. 23וַֽיְשַׁלְּחֵ֛הוּ יְהוָ֥ה אֱלֹהִ֖ים מִגַּן־עֵ֑דֶן לַֽעֲבֹד֙ אֶת־הָ֣אֲדָמָ֔ה אֲשֶׁ֥ר לֻקַּ֖ח מִשָּֽׁם׃24So he drove out the man; and he placed cherubim at the east of the garden of Eden, and a flaming sword which turned every way, to guard the way to the tree of life. 24וַיְגָ֖רֶשׁ אֶת־הָֽאָדָ֑ם וַיַּשְׁכֵּן֩ מִקֶּ֨דֶם לְגַן־עֵ֜דֶן אֶת־הַכְּרֻבִ֗ים וְאֵ֨ת לַ֤הַט הַחֶ֨רֶב֙ הַמִּתְהַפֶּ֔כֶת לִשְׁמֹ֕ר אֶת־דֶּ֖רֶךְ עֵ֥ץ הַֽחַיִּֽים׃ ס

41The man knew Eve his wife. She conceived, and gave birth to Cain, and said, “I have gotten a man with God’s help.” 1וְהָ֣אָדָ֔ם יָדַ֖ע אֶת־חַוָּ֣ה אִשְׁתּ֑וֹ וַתַּ֨הַר֙ וַתֵּ֣לֶד אֶת־קַ֔יִן וַתֹּ֕אמֶר קָנִ֥יתִי אִ֖ישׁ אֶת־יְהוָֽה׃2Again she gave birth, to Cain’s brother Abel. Abel was a keeper of sheep, but Cain was a tiller of the ground. 2וַתֹּ֣סֶף לָלֶ֔דֶת אֶת־אָחִ֖יו אֶת־הָ֑בֶל וַֽיְהִי־הֶ֨בֶל֙ רֹ֣עֵה צֹ֔אן וְקַ֕יִן הָיָ֖ה עֹבֵ֥ד אֲדָמָֽה׃3As time passed, Cain brought an offering to God from the fruit of the ground. 3וַֽיְהִ֖י מִקֵּ֣ץ יָמִ֑ים וַיָּבֵ֨א קַ֜יִן מִפְּרִ֧י הָֽאֲדָמָ֛ה מִנְחָ֖ה לַֽיהוָֽה׃4Abel also brought some of the firstborn of his flock and of its fat. God respected Abel and his offering, 4וְהֶ֨בֶל הֵבִ֥יא גַם־ה֛וּא מִבְּכֹר֥וֹת צֹאנ֖וֹ וּמֵֽחֶלְבֵהֶ֑ן וַיִּ֣שַׁע יְהוָ֔ה אֶל־הֶ֖בֶל וְאֶל־מִנְחָתֽוֹ׃5but he didn’t respect Cain and his offering. Cain was very angry, and the expression on his face fell. 5וְאֶל־קַ֥יִן וְאֶל־מִנְחָת֖וֹ לֹ֣א שָׁעָ֑ה וַיִּ֤חַר לְקַ֨יִן֙ מְאֹ֔ד וַֽיִּפְּל֖וּ פָּנָֽיו׃6God said to Cain, “Why are you angry? Why has the expression of your face fallen? 6וַיֹּ֥אמֶר יְהוָ֖ה אֶל־קָ֑יִן לָ֚מָּה חָ֣רָה לָ֔ךְ וְלָ֖מָּה נָפְל֥וּ פָנֶֽיךָ׃7If you do well, won’t it be lifted up? If you don’t do well, sin crouches at the door. Its desire is for you, but you are to rule over it.” 7הֲל֤וֹא אִם־תֵּיטִיב֙ שְׂאֵ֔ת וְאִם֙ לֹ֣א תֵיטִ֔יב לַפֶּ֖תַח חַטָּ֣את רֹבֵ֑ץ וְאֵלֶ֨יךָ֙ תְּשׁ֣וּקָת֔וֹ וְאַתָּ֖ה תִּמְשָׁל־בּֽוֹ׃8Cain said to Abel, his brother, “Let’s go into the field.” While they were in the field, Cain rose up against Abel, his brother, and killed him. 8וַיֹּ֥אמֶר קַ֖יִן אֶל־הֶ֣בֶל אָחִ֑יו וַֽיְהִי֙ בִּהְיוֹתָ֣ם בַּשָּׂדֶ֔ה וַיָּ֥קָם קַ֛יִן אֶל־הֶ֥בֶל אָחִ֖יו וַיַּהַרְגֵֽהוּ׃9God said to Cain, “Where is Abel, your brother?” He said, “I don’t know. Am I my brother’s keeper?” 9וַיֹּ֤אמֶר יְהוָה֙ אֶל־קַ֔יִן אֵ֖י הֶ֣בֶל אָחִ֑יךָ וַיֹּ֨אמֶר֙ לֹ֣א יָדַ֔עְתִּי הֲשֹׁמֵ֥ר אָחִ֖י אָנֹֽכִי׃10God said, “What have you done? The voice of your brother’s blood cries to me from the ground. 10וַיֹּ֖אמֶר מֶ֣ה עָשִׂ֑יתָ ק֚וֹל דְּמֵ֣י אָחִ֔יךָ צֹעֲקִ֥ים אֵלַ֖י מִן־הָֽאֲדָמָֽה׃11Now you are cursed because of the ground, which has opened its mouth to receive your brother’s blood from your hand. 11וְעַתָּ֖ה אָר֣וּר אָ֑תָּה מִן־הָֽאֲדָמָה֙ אֲשֶׁ֣ר פָּצְתָ֣ה אֶת־פִּ֔יהָ לָקַ֛חַת אֶת־דְּמֵ֥י אָחִ֖יךָ מִיָּדֶֽךָ׃12From now on, when you till the ground, it won’t yield its strength to you. You will be a fugitive and a wanderer in the earth.” 12כִּ֤י תַֽעֲבֹד֙ אֶת־הָ֣אֲדָמָ֔ה לֹֽא־תֹסֵ֥ף תֵּת־כֹּחָ֖הּ לָ֑ךְ נָ֥ע וָנָ֖ד תִּֽהְיֶ֥ה בָאָֽרֶץ׃13Cain said to God, “My punishment is greater than I can bear. 13וַיֹּ֥אמֶר קַ֖יִן אֶל־יְהוָ֑ה גָּד֥וֹל עֲוֺנִ֖י מִנְּשֹֽׂא׃14Behold, you have driven me out today from the surface of the ground. I will be hidden from your face, and I will be a fugitive and a wanderer in the earth. Whoever finds me will kill me.” 14הֵן֩ גֵּרַ֨שְׁתָּ אֹתִ֜י הַיּ֗וֹם מֵעַל֙ פְּנֵ֣י הָֽאֲדָמָ֔ה וּמִפָּנֶ֖יךָ אֶסָּתֵ֑ר וְהָיִ֜יתִי נָ֤ע וָנָד֙ בָּאָ֔רֶץ וְהָיָ֥ה כָל־מֹצְאִ֖י יַֽהַרְגֵֽנִי׃15God said to him, “Therefore whoever slays Cain, vengeance will be taken on him sevenfold.” God appointed a sign for Cain, so that anyone finding him would not strike him. 15וַיֹּ֧אמֶר ל֣וֹ יְהוָ֗ה לָכֵן֙ כָּל־הֹרֵ֣ג קַ֔יִן שִׁבְעָתַ֖יִם יֻקָּ֑ם וַיָּ֨שֶׂם יְהוָ֤ה לְקַ֨יִן֙ א֔וֹת לְבִלְתִּ֥י הַכּוֹת־אֹת֖וֹ כָּל־מֹצְאֽוֹ׃16Cain left God’s presence, and lived in the land of Nod, east of Eden. 16וַיֵּ֥צֵא קַ֖יִן מִלִּפְנֵ֣י יְהוָ֑ה וַיֵּ֥שֶׁב בְּאֶֽרֶץ־נ֖וֹד קִדְמַת־עֵֽדֶן׃17Cain knew his wife. She conceived, and gave birth to Enoch. He built a city, and called the name of the city, after the name of his son, Enoch. 17וַיֵּ֤דַע קַ֨יִן֙ אֶת־אִשְׁתּ֔וֹ וַתַּ֖הַר וַתֵּ֣לֶד אֶת־חֲנ֑וֹךְ וַֽיְהִי֙ בֹּ֣נֶה עִ֔יר וַיִּקְרָא֙ שֵׁ֣ם הָעִ֔יר כְּשֵׁ֖ם בְּנ֥וֹ חֲנֽוֹךְ׃18To Enoch was born Irad. Irad became the father of Mehujael. Mehujael became the father of Methushael. Methushael became the father of Lamech. 18וַיִּוָּלֵ֤ד לַֽחֲנוֹךְ֙ אֶת־עִירָ֔ד וְעִירָ֕ד יָלַ֖ד אֶת־מְחֽוּיָאֵ֑ל וּמְחִיּיָאֵ֗ל יָלַד֙ אֶת־מְת֣וּשָׁאֵ֔ל וּמְתוּשָׁאֵ֖ל יָלַ֥ד אֶת־לָֽמֶךְ׃19Lamech took two wives: the name of the first one was Adah, and the name of the second one was Zillah. 19וַיִּֽקַּֽח־ל֥וֹ לֶ֖מֶךְ שְׁתֵּ֣י נָשִׁ֑ים שֵׁ֤ם הָֽאַחַת֙ עָדָ֔ה וְשֵׁ֥ם הַשֵּׁנִ֖ית צִלָּֽה׃20Adah gave birth to Jabal, who was the father of those who dwell in tents and have livestock. 20וַתֵּ֥לֶד עָדָ֖ה אֶת־יָבָ֑ל ה֣וּא הָיָ֔ה אֲבִ֕י יֹשֵׁ֥ב אֹ֖הֶל וּמִקְנֶֽה׃21His brother’s name was Jubal, who was the father of all who handle the harp and pipe. 21וְשֵׁ֥ם אָחִ֖יו יוּבָ֑ל ה֣וּא הָיָ֔ה אֲבִ֕י כָּל־תֹּפֵ֥שׂ כִּנּ֖וֹר וְעוּגָֽב׃22Zillah also gave birth to Tubal Cain, the forger of every cutting instrument of brass. Tubal Cain’s sister was Naamah. 22וְצִלָּ֣ה גַם־הִ֗וא יָֽלְדָה֙ אֶת־תּ֣וּבַל קַ֔יִן לֹטֵ֕שׁ כָּל־חֹרֵ֥שׁ נְחֹ֖שֶׁת וּבַרְזֶ֑ל וַֽאֲח֥וֹת תּֽוּבַל־קַ֖יִן נַֽעֲמָֽה׃23Lamech said to his wives, “Adah and Zillah, hear my voice. You wives of Lamech, listen to my speech, for I have slain a man for wounding me, a young man for bruising me. 23וַיֹּ֨אמֶר לֶ֜מֶךְ לְנָשָׁ֗יו עָדָ֤ה וְצִלָּה֙ שְׁמַ֣עַן קוֹלִ֔י נְשֵׁ֣י לֶ֔מֶךְ הַאְזֵ֖נָּה אִמְרָתִ֑י כִּ֣י אִ֤ישׁ הָרַ֨גְתִּי֙ לְפִצְעִ֔י וְיֶ֖לֶד לְחַבֻּרָתִֽי׃24If Cain will be avenged seven times, truly Lamech seventy-seven times.” 24כִּ֥י שִׁבְעָתַ֖יִם יֻקַּם־קָ֑יִן וְלֶ֖מֶךְ שִׁבְעִ֥ים וְשִׁבְעָֽה׃25Adam knew his wife again. She gave birth to a son, and named him Seth, saying, “for God has given me another child instead of Abel, for Cain killed him.” 25וַיֵּ֨דַע אָדָ֥ם עוֹד֙ אֶת־אִשְׁתּ֔וֹ וַתֵּ֣לֶד בֵּ֔ן וַתִּקְרָ֥א אֶת־שְׁמ֖וֹ שֵׁ֑ת כִּ֣י שָֽׁת־לִ֤י אֱלֹהִים֙ זֶ֣רַע אַחֵ֔ר תַּ֣חַת הֶ֔בֶל כִּ֥י הֲרָג֖וֹ קָֽיִן׃26A son was also born to Seth, and he named him Enosh. 26וּלְשֵׁ֤ת גַּם־הוּא֙ יֻלַּד־בֵּ֔ן וַיִּקְרָ֥א אֶת־שְׁמ֖וֹ אֱנ֑וֹשׁ אָ֣ז הוּחַ֔ל לִקְרֹ֖א בְּשֵׁ֥ם יְהוָֽה׃ פ

5Framework1This is the book of the generations of Adam. In the day that God created man, he made him in God’s likeness. Framework1זֶ֣ה סֵ֔פֶר תּוֹלְדֹ֖ת אָדָ֑ם בְּי֗וֹם בְּרֹ֤א אֱלֹהִים֙ אָדָ֔ם בִּדְמ֥וּת אֱלֹהִ֖ים עָשָׂ֥ה אֹתֽוֹ׃2He created them male and female, and blessed them. On the day they were created, he named them Adam. 2זָכָ֥ר וּנְקֵבָ֖ה בְּרָאָ֑ם וַיְבָ֣רֶךְ אֹתָ֗ם וַיִּקְרָ֤א אֶת־שְׁמָם֙ אָדָ֔ם בְּי֖וֹם הִבָּֽרְאָֽם׃ ס3Adam lived one hundred thirty years, and became the father of a son in his own likeness, after his image, and named him Seth. 3וַֽיְחִ֣י אָדָ֗ם שְׁלֹשִׁ֤ים וּמְאַת֙ שָׁנָ֔ה וַיּ֥וֹלֶד בִּדְמוּת֖וֹ כְּצַלְמ֑וֹ וַיִּקְרָ֥א אֶת־שְׁמ֖וֹ שֵֽׁת׃4The days of Adam after he became the father of Seth were eight hundred years, and he became the father of other sons and daughters. 4וַיִּֽהְי֣וּ יְמֵי־אָדָ֗ם אַֽחֲרֵי֙ הוֹלִיד֣וֹ אֶת־שֵׁ֔ת שְׁמֹנֶ֥ה מֵאֹ֖ת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיּ֥וֹלֶד בָּנִ֖ים וּבָנֽוֹת׃5All the days that Adam lived were nine hundred thirty years, then he died. 5וַיִּֽהְי֞וּ כָּל־יְמֵ֤י אָדָם֙ אֲשֶׁר־חַ֔י תְּשַׁ֤ע מֵאוֹת֙ שָׁנָ֔ה וּשְׁלֹשִׁ֖ים שָׁנָ֑ה וַיָּמֹֽת׃ ס6Seth lived one hundred five years, then became the father of Enosh. 6וַֽיְחִי־שֵׁ֕ת חָמֵ֥שׁ שָׁנִ֖ים וּמְאַ֣ת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיּ֖וֹלֶד אֶת־אֱנֽוֹשׁ׃7Seth lived after he became the father of Enosh eight hundred seven years, and became the father of other sons and daughters. 7וַֽיְחִי־שֵׁ֗ת אַֽחֲרֵי֙ הוֹלִיד֣וֹ אֶת־אֱנ֔וֹשׁ שֶׁ֣בַע שָׁנִ֔ים וּשְׁמֹנֶ֥ה מֵא֖וֹת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיּ֥וֹלֶד בָּנִ֖ים וּבָנֽוֹת׃8All of the days of Seth were nine hundred twelve years, then he died. 8וַיִּֽהְיוּ֙ כָּל־יְמֵי־שֵׁ֔ת שְׁתֵּ֤ים עֶשְׂרֵה֙ שָׁנָ֔ה וּתְשַׁ֥ע מֵא֖וֹת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיָּמֹֽת׃ ס9Enosh lived ninety years, and became the father of Kenan. 9וַֽיְחִ֥י אֱנ֖וֹשׁ תִּשְׁעִ֣ים שָׁנָ֑ה וַיּ֖וֹלֶד אֶת־קֵינָֽן׃10Enosh lived after he became the father of Kenan eight hundred fifteen years, and became the father of other sons and daughters. 10וַֽיְחִ֣י אֱנ֗וֹשׁ אַֽחֲרֵי֙ הוֹלִיד֣וֹ אֶת־קֵינָ֔ן חֲמֵ֤שׁ עֶשְׂרֵה֙ שָׁנָ֔ה וּשְׁמֹנֶ֥ה מֵא֖וֹת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיּ֥וֹלֶד בָּנִ֖ים וּבָנֽוֹת׃11All of the days of Enosh were nine hundred five years, then he died. 11וַיִּֽהְיוּ֙ כָּל־יְמֵ֣י אֱנ֔וֹשׁ חָמֵ֣שׁ שָׁנִ֔ים וּתְשַׁ֥ע מֵא֖וֹת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיָּמֹֽת׃ ס12Kenan lived seventy years, then became the father of Mahalalel. 12וַֽיְחִ֥י קֵינָ֖ן שִׁבְעִ֣ים שָׁנָ֑ה וַיּ֖וֹלֶד אֶת־מַֽהֲלַלְאֵֽל׃13Kenan lived after he became the father of Mahalalel eight hundred forty years, and became the father of other sons and daughters 13וַיְחִ֣י קֵינָ֗ן אַחֲרֵי֙ הוֹלִיד֣וֹ אֶת־מַֽהֲלַלְאֵ֔ל אַרְבָּעִ֣ים שָׁנָ֔ה וּשְׁמֹנֶ֥ה מֵא֖וֹת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיּ֥וֹלֶד בָּנִ֖ים וּבָנֽוֹת׃14and all of the days of Kenan were nine hundred ten years, then he died. 14וַיִּֽהְיוּ֙ כָּל־יְמֵ֣י קֵינָ֔ן עֶ֣שֶׂר שָׁנִ֔ים וּתְשַׁ֥ע מֵא֖וֹת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיָּמֹֽת׃ ס15Mahalalel lived sixty-five years, then became the father of Jared. 15וַֽיְחִ֣י מַֽהֲלַלְאֵ֔ל חָמֵ֥שׁ שָׁנִ֖ים וְשִׁשִּׁ֣ים שָׁנָ֑ה וַיּ֖וֹלֶד אֶת־יָֽרֶד׃16Mahalalel lived after he became the father of Jared eight hundred thirty years, and became the father of other sons and daughters. 16וַֽיְחִ֣י מַֽהֲלַלְאֵ֗ל אַֽחֲרֵי֙ הוֹלִיד֣וֹ אֶת־יֶ֔רֶד שְׁלֹשִׁ֣ים שָׁנָ֔ה וּשְׁמֹנֶ֥ה מֵא֖וֹת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיּ֥וֹלֶד בָּנִ֖ים וּבָנֽוֹת׃17All of the days of Mahalalel were eight hundred ninety-five years, then he died. 17וַיִּהְיוּ֙ כָּל־יְמֵ֣י מַהֲלַלְאֵ֔ל חָמֵ֤שׁ וְתִשְׁעִים֙ שָׁנָ֔ה וּשְׁמֹנֶ֥ה מֵא֖וֹת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיָּמֹֽת׃ ס18Jared lived one hundred sixty-two years, then became the father of Enoch. 18וַֽיְחִי־יֶ֕רֶד שְׁתַּ֧יִם וְשִׁשִּׁ֛ים שָׁנָ֖ה וּמְאַ֣ת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיּ֖וֹלֶד אֶת־חֲנֽוֹךְ׃19Jared lived after he became the father of Enoch eight hundred years, and became the father of other sons and daughters. 19וַֽיְחִי־יֶ֗רֶד אַֽחֲרֵי֙ הוֹלִיד֣וֹ אֶת־חֲנ֔וֹךְ שְׁמֹנֶ֥ה מֵא֖וֹת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיּ֥וֹלֶד בָּנִ֖ים וּבָנֽוֹת׃20All of the days of Jared were nine hundred sixty-two years, then he died. 20וַיִּֽהְיוּ֙ כָּל־יְמֵי־יֶ֔רֶד שְׁתַּ֤יִם וְשִׁשִּׁים֙ שָׁנָ֔ה וּתְשַׁ֥ע מֵא֖וֹת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיָּמֹֽת׃ פ21Enoch lived sixty-five years, then became the father of Methuselah. 21וַֽיְחִ֣י חֲנ֔וֹךְ חָמֵ֥שׁ וְשִׁשִּׁ֖ים שָׁנָ֑ה וַיּ֖וֹלֶד אֶת־מְתוּשָֽׁלַח׃22After Methuselah’s birth, Enoch walked with God for three hundred years, and became the father of more sons and daughters. 22וַיִּתְהַלֵּ֨ךְ חֲנ֜וֹךְ אֶת־הָֽאֱלֹהִ֗ים אַֽחֲרֵי֙ הוֹלִיד֣וֹ אֶת־מְתוּשֶׁ֔לַח שְׁלֹ֥שׁ מֵא֖וֹת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיּ֥וֹלֶד בָּנִ֖ים וּבָנֽוֹת׃23All the days of Enoch were three hundred sixty-five years. 23וַיְהִ֖י כָּל־יְמֵ֣י חֲנ֑וֹךְ חָמֵ֤שׁ וְשִׁשִּׁים֙ שָׁנָ֔ה וּשְׁלֹ֥שׁ מֵא֖וֹת שָׁנָֽה׃24Enoch walked with God, and he was not found, for God took him. 24וַיִּתְהַלֵּ֥ךְ חֲנ֖וֹךְ אֶת־הָֽאֱלֹהִ֑ים וְאֵינֶ֕נּוּ כִּֽי־לָקַ֥ח אֹת֖וֹ אֱלֹהִֽים׃ פ25Methuselah lived one hundred eighty-seven years, then became the father of Lamech. 25וַיְחִ֣י מְתוּשֶׁ֔לַח שֶׁ֧בַע וּשְׁמֹנִ֛ים שָׁנָ֖ה וּמְאַ֣ת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיּ֖וֹלֶד אֶת־לָֽמֶךְ׃26Methuselah lived after he became the father of Lamech seven hundred eighty-two years, and became the father of other sons and daughters. 26וַֽיְחִ֣י מְתוּשֶׁ֗לַח אַֽחֲרֵי֙ הוֹלִיד֣וֹ אֶת־לֶ֔מֶךְ שְׁתַּ֤יִם וּשְׁמוֹנִים֙ שָׁנָ֔ה וּשְׁבַ֥ע מֵא֖וֹת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיּ֥וֹלֶד בָּנִ֖ים וּבָנֽוֹת׃27All the days of Methuselah were nine hundred sixty-nine years, then he died. 27וַיִּהְיוּ֙ כָּל־יְמֵ֣י מְתוּשֶׁ֔לַח תֵּ֤שַׁע וְשִׁשִּׁים֙ שָׁנָ֔ה וּתְשַׁ֥ע מֵא֖וֹת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיָּמֹֽת׃ פ28Lamech lived one hundred eighty-two years, then became the father of a son. 28וַֽיְחִי־לֶ֕מֶךְ שְׁתַּ֧יִם וּשְׁמֹנִ֛ים שָׁנָ֖ה וּמְאַ֣ת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיּ֖וֹלֶד בֵּֽן׃29Lamech fathered a son. He named him Noah, saying, “This one will comfort us in our work and in the toil of our hands, caused by the ground which God has cursed.” 29וַיִּקְרָ֧א אֶת־שְׁמ֛וֹ נֹ֖חַ לֵאמֹ֑ר זֶ֞֠ה יְנַחֲמֵ֤נוּ מִֽמַּעֲשֵׂ֨נוּ֙ וּמֵעִצְּב֣וֹן יָדֵ֔ינוּ מִן־הָ֣אֲדָמָ֔ה אֲשֶׁ֥ר אֵֽרְרָ֖הּ יְהוָֽה׃Framework30Lamech lived after he became the father of Noah five hundred ninety-five years, and became the father of other sons and daughters. Framework30וַֽיְחִי־לֶ֗מֶךְ אַֽחֲרֵי֙ הוֹלִיד֣וֹ אֶת־נֹ֔חַ חָמֵ֤שׁ וְתִשְׁעִים֙ שָׁנָ֔ה וַחֲמֵ֥שׁ מֵאֹ֖ת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיּ֥וֹלֶד בָּנִ֖ים וּבָנֽוֹת׃31All the days of Lamech were seven hundred seventy-seven years, then he died. 31וַֽיְהִי֙ כָּל־יְמֵי־לֶ֔מֶךְ שֶׁ֤בַע וְשִׁבְעִים֙ שָׁנָ֔ה וּשְׁבַ֥ע מֵא֖וֹת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיָּמֹֽת׃ ס32Noah fathered Shem, Ham, and Japheth. 32וַֽיְהִי־נֹ֕חַ בֶּן־חֲמֵ֥שׁ מֵא֖וֹת שָׁנָ֑ה וַיּ֣וֹלֶד נֹ֔חַ אֶת־שֵׁ֖ם אֶת־חָ֥ם וְאֶת־יָֽפֶת׃

61When men began to multiply on the surface of the ground, and daughters were born to them, 1וַֽיְהִי֙ כִּֽי־הֵחֵ֣ל הָֽאָדָ֔ם לָרֹ֖ב עַל־פְּנֵ֣י הָֽאֲדָמָ֑ה וּבָנ֖וֹת יֻלְּד֥וּ לָהֶֽם׃2God’s sons saw that men’s daughters were beautiful, and they took any that they wanted for themselves as wives. 2וַיִּרְא֤וּ בְנֵי־הָֽאֱלֹהִים֙ אֶת־בְּנ֣וֹת הָֽאָדָ֔ם כִּ֥י טֹבֹ֖ת הֵ֑נָּה וַיִּקְח֤וּ לָהֶם֙ נָשִׁ֔ים מִכֹּ֖ל אֲשֶׁ֥ר בָּחָֽרוּ׃3God said, “My Spirit will not strive with man forever, because he also is flesh; so his days will be one hundred twenty years.” 3וַיֹּ֣אמֶר יְהוָ֗ה לֹֽא־יָד֨וֹן רוּחִ֤י בָֽאָדָם֙ לְעֹלָ֔ם בְּשַׁגַּ֖ם ה֣וּא בָשָׂ֑ר וְהָי֣וּ יָמָ֔יו מֵאָ֥ה וְעֶשְׂרִ֖ים שָׁנָֽה׃4The Nephilim were in the earth in those days, and also after that, when God’s sons came in to men’s daughters and had children with them. Those were the mighty men who were of old, men of renown. 4הַנְּפִלִ֞ים הָי֣וּ בָאָרֶץ֮ בַּיָּמִ֣ים הָהֵם֒ וְגַ֣ם אַֽחֲרֵי־כֵ֗ן אֲשֶׁ֨ר יָבֹ֜אוּ בְּנֵ֤י הָֽאֱלֹהִים֙ אֶל־בְּנ֣וֹת הָֽאָדָ֔ם וְיָלְד֖וּ לָהֶ֑ם הֵ֧מָּה הַגִּבֹּרִ֛ים אֲשֶׁ֥ר מֵעוֹלָ֖ם אַנְשֵׁ֥י הַשֵּֽׁם׃ פ5God saw that the wickedness of man was great in the earth, and that every imagination of the thoughts of man’s heart was continually only evil. 5וַיַּ֣רְא יְהוָ֔ה כִּ֥י רַבָּ֛ה רָעַ֥ת הָאָדָ֖ם בָּאָ֑רֶץ וְכָל־יֵ֨צֶר֙ מַחְשְׁבֹ֣ת לִבּ֔וֹ רַ֥ק רַ֖ע כָּל־הַיּֽוֹם׃6God was sorry that he had made man on the earth, and it grieved him in his heart. 6וַיִּנָּ֣חֶם יְהוָ֔ה כִּֽי־עָשָׂ֥ה אֶת־הָֽאָדָ֖ם בָּאָ֑רֶץ וַיִּתְעַצֵּ֖ב אֶל־לִבּֽוֹ׃7God said, “I will destroy man whom I have created from the surface of the ground—man, along with animals, creeping things, and birds of the sky—for I am sorry that I have made them.” 7וַיֹּ֣אמֶר יְהוָ֗ה אֶמְחֶ֨ה אֶת־הָאָדָ֤ם אֲשֶׁר־בָּרָ֨אתִי֙ מֵעַל֙ פְּנֵ֣י הָֽאֲדָמָ֔ה מֵֽאָדָם֙ עַד־בְּהֵמָ֔ה עַד־רֶ֖מֶשׂ וְעַד־ע֣וֹף הַשָּׁמָ֑יִם כִּ֥י נִחַ֖מְתִּי כִּ֥י עֲשִׂיתִֽם׃8But Noah found favor in God’s eyes. 8וְנֹ֕חַ מָ֥צָא חֵ֖ן בְּעֵינֵ֥י יְהוָֽה׃ פPriestly9This is the history of the generations of Noah: Noah was a righteous man, blameless among the people of his time. Noah walked with God. Priestly9אֵ֚לֶּה תּוֹלְדֹ֣ת נֹ֔חַ נֹ֗חַ אִ֥ישׁ צַדִּ֛יק תָּמִ֥ים הָיָ֖ה בְּדֹֽרֹתָ֑יו אֶת־הָֽאֱלֹהִ֖ים הִֽתְהַלֶּךְ־נֹֽחַ׃10Noah became the father of three sons: Shem, Ham, and Japheth. 10וַיּ֥וֹלֶד נֹ֖חַ שְׁלֹשָׁ֣ה בָנִ֑ים אֶת־שֵׁ֖ם אֶת־חָ֥ם וְאֶת־יָֽפֶת׃11The earth was corrupt before God, and the earth was filled with violence. 11וַתִּשָּׁחֵ֥ת הָאָ֖רֶץ לִפְנֵ֣י הָֽאֱלֹהִ֑ים וַתִּמָּלֵ֥א הָאָ֖רֶץ חָמָֽס׃12God saw the earth, and saw that it was corrupt, for all flesh had corrupted their way on the earth. 12וַיַּ֧רְא אֱלֹהִ֛ים אֶת־הָאָ֖רֶץ וְהִנֵּ֣ה נִשְׁחָ֑תָה כִּֽי־הִשְׁחִ֧ית כָּל־בָּשָׂ֛ר אֶת־דַּרְכּ֖וֹ עַל־הָאָֽרֶץ׃ ס13God said to Noah, “I will bring an end to all flesh, for the earth is filled with violence through them. Behold, I will destroy them and the earth. 13וַיֹּ֨אמֶר אֱלֹהִ֜ים לְנֹ֗חַ קֵ֤ץ כָּל־בָּשָׂר֙ בָּ֣א לְפָנַ֔י כִּֽי־מָלְאָ֥ה הָאָ֛רֶץ חָמָ֖ס מִפְּנֵיהֶ֑ם וְהִנְנִ֥י מַשְׁחִיתָ֖ם אֶת־הָאָֽרֶץ׃14Make a ship of gopher wood. You shall make rooms in the ship, and shall seal it inside and outside with pitch. 14עֲשֵׂ֤ה לְךָ֙ תֵּבַ֣ת עֲצֵי־גֹ֔פֶר קִנִּ֖ים תַּֽעֲשֶׂ֣ה אֶת־הַתֵּבָ֑ה וְכָֽפַרְתָּ֥ אֹתָ֛הּ מִבַּ֥יִת וּמִח֖וּץ בַּכֹּֽפֶר׃15This is how you shall make it. The length of the ship shall be three hundred cubits, its width fifty cubits, and its height thirty cubits. 15וְזֶ֕ה אֲשֶׁ֥ר תַּֽעֲשֶׂ֖ה אֹתָ֑הּ שְׁלֹ֧שׁ מֵא֣וֹת אַמָּ֗ה אֹ֚רֶךְ הַתֵּבָ֔ה חֲמִשִּׁ֤ים אַמָּה֙ רָחְבָּ֔הּ וּשְׁלֹשִׁ֥ים אַמָּ֖ה קוֹמָתָֽהּ׃16You shall make a roof in the ship, and you shall finish it to a cubit upward. You shall set the door of the ship in its side. You shall make it with lower, second, and third levels. 16צֹ֣הַר ׀ תַּֽעֲשֶׂ֣ה לַתֵּבָ֗ה וְאֶל־אַמָּה֙ תְּכַלֶ֣נָּה מִלְמַ֔עְלָה וּפֶ֥תַח הַתֵּבָ֖ה בְּצִדָּ֣הּ תָּשִׂ֑ים תַּחְתִּיִּ֛ם שְׁנִיִּ֥ם וּשְׁלִשִׁ֖ים תַּֽעֲשֶֽׂהָ׃Priestly17I, even I, will bring the flood of waters on this earth, to destroy all flesh having the breath of life from under the sky. Everything that is in the earth will die. Priestly17וַאֲנִ֗י הִנְנִי֩ מֵבִ֨יא אֶת־הַמַּבּ֥וּל מַ֨יִם֙ עַל־הָאָ֔רֶץ לְשַׁחֵ֣ת כָּל־בָּשָׂ֗ר אֲשֶׁר־בּוֹ֙ ר֣וּחַ חַיִּ֔ים מִתַּ֖חַת הַשָּׁמָ֑יִם כֹּ֥ל אֲשֶׁר־בָּאָ֖רֶץ יִגְוָֽע׃18But I will establish my covenant with you. You shall come into the ship, you, your sons, your wife, and your sons’ wives with you. 18וַהֲקִמֹתִ֥י אֶת־בְּרִיתִ֖י אִתָּ֑ךְ וּבָאתָ֙ אֶל־הַתֵּבָ֔ה אַתָּ֕ה וּבָנֶ֛יךָ וְאִשְׁתְּךָ֥ וּנְשֵֽׁי־בָנֶ֖יךָ אִתָּֽךְ׃19Of every living thing of all flesh, you shall bring two of every sort into the ship, to keep them alive with you. They shall be male and female. 19וּמִכָּל־הָ֠חַי מִֽכָּל־בָּשָׂ֞ר שְׁנַ֧יִם מִכֹּ֛ל תָּבִ֥יא אֶל־הַתֵּבָ֖ה לְהַחֲיֹ֣ת אִתָּ֑ךְ זָכָ֥ר וּנְקֵבָ֖ה יִֽהְיֽוּ׃20Of the birds after their kind, of the livestock after their kind, of every creeping thing of the ground after its kind, two of every sort will come to you, to keep them alive. 20מֵהָע֣וֹף לְמִינֵ֗הוּ וּמִן־הַבְּהֵמָה֙ לְמִינָ֔הּ מִכֹּ֛ל רֶ֥מֶשׂ הָֽאֲדָמָ֖ה לְמִינֵ֑הוּ שְׁנַ֧יִם מִכֹּ֛ל יָבֹ֥אוּ אֵלֶ֖יךָ לְהַֽחֲיֽוֹת׃21Take with you some of all food that is eaten, and gather it to yourself; and it will be for food for you, and for them.” 21וְאַתָּ֣ה קַח־לְךָ֗ מִכָּל־מַֽאֲכָל֙ אֲשֶׁ֣ר יֵֽאָכֵ֔ל וְאָסַפְתָּ֖ אֵלֶ֑יךָ וְהָיָ֥ה לְךָ֛ וְלָהֶ֖ם לְאָכְלָֽה׃22Thus Noah did. He did all that God commanded him. 22וַיַּ֖עַשׂ נֹ֑חַ כְּ֠כֹל אֲשֶׁ֨ר צִוָּ֥ה אֹת֛וֹ אֱלֹהִ֖ים כֵּ֥ן עָשָֽׂה׃ ס

71God said to Noah, “Come with all of your household into the ship, for I have seen your righteousness before me in this generation. 1וַיֹּ֤אמֶר יְהוָה֙ לְנֹ֔חַ בֹּֽא־אַתָּ֥ה וְכָל־בֵּיתְךָ֖ אֶל־הַתֵּבָ֑ה כִּֽי־אֹתְךָ֥ רָאִ֛יתִי צַדִּ֥יק לְפָנַ֖י בַּדּ֥וֹר הַזֶּֽה׃2You shall take seven pairs of every animal with you, the male and his female. 2מִכֹּ֣ל ׀ הַבְּהֵמָ֣ה הַטְּהוֹרָ֗ה תִּֽקַּח־לְךָ֛ שִׁבְעָ֥ה שִׁבְעָ֖ה אִ֣ישׁ וְאִשְׁתּ֑וֹ וּמִן־הַבְּהֵמָ֡ה אֲ֠שֶׁר לֹ֣א טְהֹרָ֥ה הִ֛וא שְׁנַ֖יִם אִ֥ישׁ וְאִשְׁתּֽוֹ׃3Also of the birds of the sky, seven and seven, male and female, to keep seed alive on the surface of all the earth. 3גַּ֣ם מֵע֧וֹף הַשָּׁמַ֛יִם שִׁבְעָ֥ה שִׁבְעָ֖ה זָכָ֣ר וּנְקֵבָ֑ה לְחַיּ֥וֹת זֶ֖רַע עַל־פְּנֵ֥י כָל־הָאָֽרֶץ׃4In seven days, I will cause it to rain on the earth for forty days and forty nights. I will destroy every living thing that I have made from the surface of the ground.” 4כִּי֩ לְיָמִ֨ים ע֜וֹד שִׁבְעָ֗ה אָֽנֹכִי֙ מַמְטִ֣יר עַל־הָאָ֔רֶץ אַרְבָּעִ֣ים י֔וֹם וְאַרְבָּעִ֖ים לָ֑יְלָה וּמָחִ֗יתִי אֶֽת־כָּל־הַיְקוּם֙ אֲשֶׁ֣ר עָשִׂ֔יתִי מֵעַ֖ל פְּנֵ֥י הָֽאֲדָמָֽה׃5Noah did everything that God commanded him. 5וַיַּ֖עַשׂ נֹ֑חַ כְּכֹ֥ל אֲשֶׁר־צִוָּ֖הוּ יְהוָֽה׃Priestly6Noah was six hundred years old when the flood of waters came on the earth. Priestly6וְנֹ֕חַ בֶּן־שֵׁ֥שׁ מֵא֖וֹת שָׁנָ֑ה וְהַמַּבּ֣וּל הָיָ֔ה מַ֖יִם עַל־הָאָֽרֶץ׃7Noah went into the ship with his sons, his wife, and his sons’ wives, because of the floodwaters. 7וַיָּ֣בֹא נֹ֗חַ וּ֠בָנָיו וְאִשְׁתּ֧וֹ וּנְשֵֽׁי־בָנָ֛יו אִתּ֖וֹ אֶל־הַתֵּבָ֑ה מִפְּנֵ֖י מֵ֥י הַמַּבּֽוּל׃8Animals, birds, and everything that creeps on the ground 8מִן־הַבְּהֵמָה֙ הַטְּהוֹרָ֔ה וּמִן־הַ֨בְּהֵמָ֔ה אֲשֶׁ֥ר אֵינֶ֖נָּה טְהֹרָ֑ה וּמִ֨ן־הָע֔וֹף וְכֹ֥ל אֲשֶׁר־רֹמֵ֖שׂ עַל־הָֽאֲדָמָֽה׃9went to Noah into the ship, male and female, as God commanded Noah. 9שְׁנַ֨יִם שְׁנַ֜יִם בָּ֧אוּ אֶל־נֹ֛חַ אֶל־הַתֵּבָ֖ה זָכָ֣ר וּנְקֵבָ֑ה כַּֽאֲשֶׁ֛ר צִוָּ֥ה אֱלֹהִ֖ים אֶת־נֹֽחַ׃10After the seven days, the floodwaters came on the earth. 10וַֽיְהִ֖י לְשִׁבְעַ֣ת הַיָּמִ֑ים וּמֵ֣י הַמַּבּ֔וּל הָי֖וּ עַל־הָאָֽרֶץ׃Priestly11In the six hundredth year of Noah’s life, in the second month, on the seventeenth day of the month, on that day all the fountains of the great deep burst open, and the sky’s windows opened. Priestly11בִּשְׁנַ֨ת שֵׁשׁ־מֵא֤וֹת שָׁנָה֙ לְחַיֵּי־נֹ֔חַ בַּחֹ֨דֶשׁ֙ הַשֵּׁנִ֔י בְּשִׁבְעָֽה־עָשָׂ֥ר י֖וֹם לַחֹ֑דֶשׁ בַּיּ֣וֹם הַזֶּ֗ה נִבְקְעוּ֙ כָּֽל־מַעְיְנֹת֙ תְּה֣וֹם רַבָּ֔ה וַאֲרֻבֹּ֥ת הַשָּׁמַ֖יִם נִפְתָּֽחוּ׃12It rained on the earth forty days and forty nights. 12וַֽיְהִ֥י הַגֶּ֖שֶׁם עַל־הָאָ֑רֶץ אַרְבָּעִ֣ים י֔וֹם וְאַרְבָּעִ֖ים לָֽיְלָה׃Priestly13In the same day Noah, and Shem, Ham, and Japheth—the sons of Noah—and Noah’s wife and the three wives of his sons with them, entered into the ship— Priestly13בְּעֶ֨צֶם הַיּ֤וֹם הַזֶּה֙ בָּ֣א נֹ֔חַ וְשֵׁם־וְחָ֥ם וָיֶ֖פֶת בְּנֵי־נֹ֑חַ וְאֵ֣שֶׁת נֹ֗חַ וּשְׁלֹ֧שֶׁת נְשֵֽׁי־בָנָ֛יו אִתָּ֖ם אֶל־הַתֵּבָֽה׃14they, and every animal after its kind, all the livestock after their kind, every creeping thing that creeps on the earth after its kind, and every bird after its kind, every bird of every sort. 14הֵ֜מָּה וְכָל־הַֽחַיָּ֣ה לְמִינָ֗הּ וְכָל־הַבְּהֵמָה֙ לְמִינָ֔הּ וְכָל־הָרֶ֛מֶשׂ הָרֹמֵ֥שׂ עַל־הָאָ֖רֶץ לְמִינֵ֑הוּ וְכָל־הָע֣וֹף לְמִינֵ֔הוּ כֹּ֖ל צִפּ֥וֹר כָּל־כָּנָֽף׃15Pairs from all flesh with the breath of life in them went into the ship to Noah. 15וַיָּבֹ֥אוּ אֶל־נֹ֖חַ אֶל־הַתֵּבָ֑ה שְׁנַ֤יִם שְׁנַ֨יִם֙ מִכָּל־הַבָּשָׂ֔ר אֲשֶׁר־בּ֖וֹ ר֥וּחַ חַיִּֽים׃16Those who went in, went in male and female of all flesh, as God commanded him; then God shut him in. 16וְהַבָּאִ֗ים זָכָ֨ר וּנְקֵבָ֤ה מִכָּל־בָּשָׂר֙ בָּ֔אוּ כַּֽאֲשֶׁ֛ר צִוָּ֥ה אֹת֖וֹ אֱלֹהִ֑ים וַיִּסְגֹּ֥ר יְהוָ֖ה בַּֽעֲדֽוֹ׃17The flood was forty days on the earth. The waters increased, and lifted up the ship, and it was lifted up above the earth. 17וַֽיְהִ֧י הַמַּבּ֛וּל אַרְבָּעִ֥ים י֖וֹם עַל־הָאָ֑רֶץ וַיִּרְבּ֣וּ הַמַּ֗יִם וַיִּשְׂאוּ֙ אֶת־הַתֵּבָ֔ה וַתָּ֖רָם מֵעַ֥ל הָאָֽרֶץ׃Priestly18The waters rose, and increased greatly on the earth; and the ship floated on the surface of the waters. Priestly18וַיִּגְבְּר֥וּ הַמַּ֛יִם וַיִּרְבּ֥וּ מְאֹ֖ד עַל־הָאָ֑רֶץ וַתֵּ֥לֶךְ הַתֵּבָ֖ה עַל־פְּנֵ֥י הַמָּֽיִם׃19The waters rose very high on the earth. All the high mountains that were under the whole sky were covered. 19וְהַמַּ֗יִם גָּֽבְר֛וּ מְאֹ֥ד מְאֹ֖ד עַל־הָאָ֑רֶץ וַיְכֻסּ֗וּ כָּל־הֶֽהָרִים֙ הַגְּבֹהִ֔ים אֲשֶׁר־תַּ֖חַת כָּל־הַשָּׁמָֽיִם׃20The waters rose fifteen cubits higher, and the mountains were covered. 20חֲמֵ֨שׁ עֶשְׂרֵ֤ה אַמָּה֙ מִלְמַ֔עְלָה גָּבְר֖וּ הַמָּ֑יִם וַיְכֻסּ֖וּ הֶהָרִֽים׃21All flesh died that moved on the earth, including birds, livestock, animals, every creeping thing that creeps on the earth, and every man. 21וַיִּגְוַ֞ע כָּל־בָּשָׂ֣ר ׀ הָרֹמֵ֣שׂ עַל־הָאָ֗רֶץ בָּע֤וֹף וּבַבְּהֵמָה֙ וּבַ֣חַיָּ֔ה וּבְכָל־הַשֶּׁ֖רֶץ הַשֹּׁרֵ֣ץ עַל־הָאָ֑רֶץ וְכֹ֖ל הָאָדָֽם׃22All on the dry land, in whose nostrils was the breath of the spirit of life, died. 22כֹּ֡ל אֲשֶׁר֩ נִשְׁמַת־ר֨וּחַ חַיִּ֜ים בְּאַפָּ֗יו מִכֹּ֛ל אֲשֶׁ֥ר בֶּחָֽרָבָ֖ה מֵֽתוּ׃23Every living thing was destroyed that was on the surface of the ground, including man, livestock, creeping things, and birds of the sky. They were destroyed from the earth. Only Noah was left, and those who were with him in the ship. 23וַיִּ֜מַח אֶֽת־כָּל־הַיְק֣וּם ׀ אֲשֶׁ֣ר ׀ עַל־פְּנֵ֣י הָֽאֲדָמָ֗ה מֵאָדָ֤ם עַד־בְּהֵמָה֙ עַד־רֶ֨מֶשׂ֙ וְעַד־ע֣וֹף הַשָּׁמַ֔יִם וַיִּמָּח֖וּ מִן־הָאָ֑רֶץ וַיִשָּׁ֧אֶר אַךְ־נֹ֛חַ וַֽאֲשֶׁ֥ר אִתּ֖וֹ בַּתֵּבָֽה׃ Priestly24The waters flooded the earth one hundred fifty days. Priestly24וַיִּגְבְּר֥וּ הַמַּ֖יִם עַל־הָאָ֑רֶץ חֲמִשִּׁ֥ים וּמְאַ֖ת יֽוֹם׃

8Priestly1God remembered Noah, all the animals, and all the livestock that were with him in the ship; and God made a wind to pass over the earth. The waters subsided. Priestly1וַיִּזְכֹּ֤ר אֱלֹהִים֙ אֶת־נֹ֔חַ וְאֵ֤ת כָּל־הַֽחַיָּה֙ וְאֶת־כָּל־הַבְּהֵמָ֔ה אֲשֶׁ֥ר אִתּ֖וֹ בַּתֵּבָ֑ה וַיַּעֲבֵ֨ר אֱלֹהִ֥ים ר֨וּחַ֙ עַל־הָאָ֔רֶץ וַיָּשֹׁ֖כּוּ הַמָּֽיִם׃2The deep’s fountains and the sky’s windows were also stopped, and the rain from the sky was restrained. 2וַיִּסָּֽכְרוּ֙ מַעְיְנֹ֣ת תְּה֔וֹם וַֽאֲרֻבֹּ֖ת הַשָּׁמָ֑יִם וַיִּכָּלֵ֥א הַגֶּ֖שֶׁם מִן־הַשָּׁמָֽיִם׃3The waters continually receded from the earth. 3וַיָּשֻׁ֧בוּ הַמַּ֛יִם מֵעַ֥ל הָאָ֖רֶץ הָל֣וֹךְ וָשׁ֑וֹב וַיַּחְסְר֣וּ הַמַּ֔יִם מִקְצֵ֕ה חֲמִשִּׁ֥ים וּמְאַ֖ת יֽוֹם׃4The ship rested on Ararat’s mountains. 4וַתָּ֤נַח הַתֵּבָה֙ בַּחֹ֣דֶשׁ הַשְּׁבִיעִ֔י בְּשִׁבְעָה־עָשָׂ֥ר י֖וֹם לַחֹ֑דֶשׁ עַ֖ל הָרֵ֥י אֲרָרָֽט׃Priestly5The waters receded continually until the tenth month. In the tenth month, on the first day of the month, the tops of the mountains were visible. Priestly5וְהַמַּ֗יִם הָיוּ֙ הָל֣וֹךְ וְחָס֔וֹר עַ֖ד הַחֹ֣דֶשׁ הָֽעֲשִׂירִ֑י בָּֽעֲשִׂירִי֙ בְּאֶחָ֣ד לַחֹ֔דֶשׁ נִרְא֖וּ רָאשֵׁ֥י הֶֽהָרִֽים׃6At the end of forty days, Noah opened the window of the ship which he had made, 6וַֽיְהִ֕י מִקֵּ֖ץ אַרְבָּעִ֣ים י֑וֹם וַיִּפְתַּ֣ח נֹ֔חַ אֶת־חַלּ֥וֹן הַתֵּבָ֖ה אֲשֶׁ֥ר עָשָֽׂה׃7and he sent out a raven. It went back and forth, until the waters were dried up from the earth. 7וַיְשַׁלַּ֖ח אֶת־הָֽעֹרֵ֑ב וַיֵּצֵ֤א יָצוֹא֙ וָשׁ֔וֹב עַד־יְבֹ֥שֶׁת הַמַּ֖יִם מֵעַ֥ל הָאָֽרֶץ׃8He himself sent out a dove to see if the waters were abated from the surface of the ground, 8וַיְשַׁלַּ֥ח אֶת־הַיּוֹנָ֖ה מֵאִתּ֑וֹ לִרְאוֹת֙ הֲקַ֣לּוּ הַמַּ֔יִם מֵעַ֖ל פְּנֵ֥י הָֽאֲדָמָֽה׃9but the dove found no place to rest her foot, and she returned into the ship to him, for the waters were on the surface of the whole earth. He put out his hand, and took her, and brought her to him into the ship. 9וְלֹֽא־מָצְאָה֩ הַיּוֹנָ֨ה מָנ֜וֹחַ לְכַף־רַגְלָ֗הּ וַתָּ֤שָׁב אֵלָיו֙ אֶל־הַתֵּבָ֔ה כִּי־מַ֖יִם עַל־פְּנֵ֣י כָל־הָאָ֑רֶץ וַיִּשְׁלַ֤ח יָדוֹ֙ וַיִּקָּחֶ֔הָ וַיָּבֵ֥א אֹתָ֛הּ אֵלָ֖יו אֶל־הַתֵּבָֽה׃10He waited yet another seven days; and again he sent the dove out of the ship. 10וַיָּ֣חֶל ע֔וֹד שִׁבְעַ֥ת יָמִ֖ים אֲחֵרִ֑ים וַיֹּ֛סֶף שַׁלַּ֥ח אֶת־הַיּוֹנָ֖ה מִן־הַתֵּבָֽה׃11The dove came back to him at evening and, behold, in her mouth was a freshly plucked olive leaf. So Noah knew that the waters were abated from the earth. 11וַתָּבֹ֨א אֵלָ֤יו הַיּוֹנָה֙ לְעֵ֣ת עֶ֔רֶב וְהִנֵּ֥ה עֲלֵה־זַ֖יִת טָרָ֣ף בְּפִ֑יהָ וַיֵּ֣דַע נֹ֔חַ כִּי־קַ֥לּוּ הַמַּ֖יִם מֵעַ֥ל הָאָֽרֶץ׃12He waited yet another seven days, and sent out the dove; and she didn’t return to him anymore. 12וַיִּיָּ֣חֶל ע֔וֹד שִׁבְעַ֥ת יָמִ֖ים אֲחֵרִ֑ים וַיְשַׁלַּח֙ אֶת־הַיּוֹנָ֔ה וְלֹֽא־יָסְפָ֥ה שׁוּב־אֵלָ֖יו עֽוֹד׃13Noah removed the covering of the ship, and looked. He saw that the surface of the ground was dried. 13וַֽ֠יְהִי בְּאַחַ֨ת וְשֵׁשׁ־מֵא֜וֹת שָׁנָ֗ה בָּֽרִאשׁוֹן֙ בְּאֶחָ֣ד לַחֹ֔דֶשׁ חָֽרְב֥וּ הַמַּ֖יִם מֵעַ֣ל הָאָ֑רֶץ וַיָּ֤סַר נֹ֨חַ֙ אֶת־מִכְסֵ֣ה הַתֵּבָ֔ה וַיַּ֕רְא וְהִנֵּ֥ה חָֽרְב֖וּ פְּנֵ֥י הָֽאֲדָמָֽה׃Priestly14In the second month, on the twenty-seventh day of the month, the earth was dry.
